# Agent inventory: goodvibes-agent to products/agent

Every tracked file of goodvibes-agent (1602 files, from `git -C ~/Projects/goodvibes-agent ls-files`), read in full, with its disposition and, for a JEV or HOIST file, its decision points. Nothing is dropped unless it is specific to WRFC (the old write, review, fix, check controller and its workstream/attempt-judging machinery) or QEMU (sandbox templates and runtime); every other DROP line the intent document states for this product is void, per the owner ruling of 2026-09-26. That ruling reaches further than the products/agent section's own explicit callout: the intent document's products/agent Config bullet also names two files, `ensure-daemon-config-migrated.ts` and `ensure-daemon-enabled-migrated.ts` (plus their test), DROP, but neither is WRFC- or QEMU-specific, so that line is void too and both PORT unchanged, as genuine one-time migration scripts with no guesswork. No file of this product is dropped: WRFC code is JEV in the contract runner, as in the engine inventory, so `src/test/runtime/conversation-continuation-contract.test.ts` (the WRFC-gated work-chain escalation) is re-expressed against contract escalations. `src/test/tools/repl.test.ts` tests the engine REPL tool's sandbox gating, which outlives the QEMU backend, so it ports (see its row). Every other WRFC-touching file in the repository, including the roughly 90 `src/input/agent-workspace-*.ts` editor files, the `src/tools/agent-delegation-tool.ts` / `agent-work-plan-tool.ts` / `agent-workspace-tool.ts` / `agent-review-packet-*.ts` family, and the many tests that construct a `WrfcController` stub or reference `wrfc`-named fields, is JEV: it carries over, submitting contract-runner verbs where it used to submit WRFC verbs, or serving as the seam a contract-runner rewrite plugs into. Sub-agents themselves (the capability of running child agents) are kept throughout; only the WRFC controller's review/fix loop and its attempt-judging state machine are retired.

Two other patterns recur across many areas and are called out once here rather than in every section: first, several products/agent HOIST bullets name a Jev reading (route planner choice, learning-curator classification, model-comparison candidate selection, personal-ops lane classification, date-parts extraction for schedules) that in practice lives in one shared module (most often `src/tools/agent-route-planner-helpers.ts` or the `@pellux/goodvibes-sdk` engine package) rather than in every file that calls it; those calling/facade files are marked HOIST or JEV with a note pointing at the file that actually holds the heuristic, rather than inventing a duplicate decision point. Second, a number of test files exercise engine-owned modules (`Orchestrator`, `WrfcController`, the memory/session spine transports, the gate's policy and exec-posture checks) that merely happen to live under this product's `src/test/` tree; those are marked HOIST or JEV to match the engine subsystem they actually test, not PORT, since the code under test is not really this product's own.

Files: 1602. PORT 1196, JEV 175, HOIST 231, DROP 0. Decision points: 105.

## Entry, renderer and shared utils

This slice covers the agent's main entry point (composition root, CLI flags, version, provider auth route display), the full terminal renderer (theme, escapes, status glyphs, primitives, layout, progress, thinking, compositor, conversation surface, overlays, markdown, code blocks, tool calls, diffs, modals), `src/utils`, and the matching tests under `src/test/renderer` and `src/test/utils`. 131 files, read in full.

This is almost entirely rendering and layout code: cell buffers, ANSI escape sequences, box drawing, scroll math, fixed markdown grammar, fixed lookup tables keyed by already-typed enums, and deterministic arithmetic (token counts, luminance, durations). None of that is a decision point. Four files genuinely guess a classification from free text rather than reading it off an already-typed field, and are marked JEV: `agent-workspace.ts` (validation-message severity from a substring check), `mcp-workspace.ts` (MCP status tone from a keyword ladder, the same shape as goodvibes-webui's already-flagged `classifyBadgeTone`), `model-workspace.ts` (a "local backend" guess from a hardcoded ten-entry provider-name list, the same shape as goodvibes-webui's already-flagged `detectFamily`), and `system-message.ts` (message severity from a bracket-prefix-plus-keyword ladder, including a WRFC-specific branch). Two test files that pin this behavior down directly, `model-workspace.test.ts` and `system-message.test.ts`, are marked JEV alongside them.

Nothing in this slice is WRFC-controller or QEMU-sandbox specific code, so there are zero DROP files. One near-miss is worth recording: `settings-modal.ts` and `settings-modal-helpers.ts` both carry a `wrfc` settings category (label "WRFC", description text about "WRFC review/fix chain scoring, fix attempts, and commit preferences"). That is fixed label and description text, not a guessed classification, so it stays PORT; the category's underlying config keys live in `src/input/settings-modal.ts` (out of this slice's scope) and will need renaming to contract-runner terms as part of the WRFC port, which is a content update, not a disposition change here. Also out of scope but worth flagging for whichever slice covers `src/input/`: `model-picker-overlay.ts` and `model-workspace.ts` in this slice consume an already-computed model group key and quality tier from `src/input/model-picker.ts` and `@pellux/goodvibes-sdk/platform/providers`; if a `detectFamily`-style vendor-name regex ladder exists in this product, it lives there, not in this slice's files.

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/renderer/agent-workspace.ts`, `editorContextLines` | `editor.message.includes('required')`, `.includes('cannot')` or `.includes('Cannot')` guesses whether a free-text editor validation message describes a blocking problem (colored warn) versus routine info, by matching three literal substrings. | A narrow yes/no reading ("does this message describe a blocking validation failure?") over the message text, per the typed-reading pattern; the editor's message-producing code passes a typed `blocking: boolean` alongside the text instead of the renderer re-guessing it from the string. |
| `src/renderer/mcp-workspace.ts`, `statusColor` | `text.includes('failed')`, `.includes('Save failed')` or `.includes('Remove failed')` maps to a bad tone; `.includes('attention')` or `.includes('quarantine')` maps to warn; else muted, all over the free-text `workspace.status` line. | A closed-choice reading over the status text, or better, the status-producing code emits a typed severity alongside the message so the renderer never re-classifies it. |
| `src/renderer/model-workspace.ts`, `modelHardwareFitLine` | A ten-entry exact-match provider-name list (`ollama`, `vllm`, `llama-cpp`, `llama.cpp`, `lm-studio`, `lmstudio`, `localai`, `tgi`, `local-openai`, `openai-compatible-local`) guessing whether a model's provider is a local inference backend, to decide whether to show a hardware-fit hint. Flagged for the same owner-ruling question raised in `docs/inventory/webui.md` for `model-catalog.ts`'s `detectFamily`: this is a display-only hint, not a routing or tier decision, so it may fall outside the "no vendor or model names in routing or tier rules" rule; if the owner reads that rule as "no vendor names anywhere," the right fix is a typed `isLocalBackend` flag rather than a Jev reading. | Either a typed `isLocalBackend` field sourced from the provider registry (deterministic, no Jev needed), or, if judgment is wanted here, a routing-battery-style yes/no reading, pending the same owner ruling raised for `detectFamily`. |
| `src/test/renderer/model-workspace.test.ts`, `describe('modelHardwareFitLine (via detailLines)')` | Pins the exact behavior of the provider-name keyword ladder above: an ollama-served model gets a "Hardware:" fit line, while groq serving the identical `llama-3.1-70b` model id does not. | Same replacement as `model-workspace.ts`'s own row: a typed `isLocalBackend` flag from the provider registry, or a Jev reading, pending the owner ruling on provider-name lists. |
| `src/renderer/system-message.ts`, `classifySystemMessage` | A multi-tier keyword and regex ladder over the message's free text: bracket-prefix dispatch (`[WRFC]` with `Gate:.*FAILED` or `FAILED`/`cascade abort` mapping to error and `spawning a fix agent` mapping to warning; `[Agents]` with failure markers mapping to error; `[Plan]` cohort-failure counts mapping to warning; `[Model]` "Unknown model" mapping to warning; `[Recovery]` "Failed to restore" mapping to error), then a generic keyword scan on quote-stripped text (`/\b(error|failed|denied|crash\w*|exception)\b/i` maps to error, `/\b(warning|context usage|caution|deprecated)\b/i` maps to warning, else info). | A narrow choice-over-fixed-options reading (error, warning or info) per the routing and classification pattern, replacing the keyword ladder; the `[WRFC]`-tagged branch specifically is renamed to whatever tag the contract runner emits for gate and fix-agent events, since WRFC itself is replaced by the contract runner. |
| `src/test/renderer/system-message.test.ts`, tests for `classifySystemMessage` | Pins the exact prefix and keyword list above directly from the test's own fixtures, including the quote-stripping behavior that keeps a quoted task description like `Task: "Fix the error in auth.ts"` from false-positiving on the word "error". | Same replacement as `system-message.ts`'s own row: the numeric cohort counts stay as code arithmetic feeding the reading rather than string-matched. |

## Core and config

## Core and config inventory: goodvibes-agent src/core and src/config to products/agent

Every file of this slice (92 files: `src/core`, `src/config`, and their matching tests under `src/test/core` and `src/test/config`, from `git -C ~/Projects/goodvibes-agent ls-files`), read in full, with its disposition and, for a JEV or HOIST file, its decision points. The intent document's Config bullet under products/agent PORT names two modules (`ensure-daemon-config-migrated.ts`, `ensure-daemon-enabled-migrated.ts`) as DROP, but neither is WRFC- or QEMU-specific, so per the owner ruling of 2026-09-26 that line is void: both are confirmed here to be genuine one-time migration scripts with no guesswork, and they PORT unchanged rather than dropping. This slice has zero DROP rows and zero true HOIST-of-product-code rows; the ten `src/test/core` files marked HOIST below are tests of engine-owned modules that merely live under this product's test tree.

Most of `src/config` is deterministic ownership/routing/schema plumbing (which runtime owns a setting, which secret tier a credential belongs in by exact key-name or prefix match against a closed, code-defined list, money detection by the schema's own `unit` mark) and stays PORT for the same reason the webui inventory's structural wire-shape checks stay PORT: matching a closed, enumerated vocabulary the code itself defines is not the same act as guessing a classification from open-ended prose. One config file is different in kind: `settings-search-vocabulary.ts` hand-maintains alias word-lists so a person's free-text settings search ("credit card") reaches the right settings domain ("payments"); this is exactly the same shape of problem as the webui reference's `classifyBadgeTone`/`detectFamily` heuristics, an open-ended text-to-closed-set guess, and it is marked JEV.

Most of `src/core` is deterministic transcript/state rendering over already-typed data (turn structure, fold, snapshot restore, hardware-fit arithmetic, thinking-overlay clocks) and stays PORT. Two files guess a classification from message text rather than dispatching on a typed field: `activity-feed.ts`'s `classifyActivityKind` (a keyword/regex ladder sorting an arbitrary message into one of 7 activity buckets) and `system-message-noise.ts` (a regex ladder deciding whether an operational message is noise, worth folding, or worth surfacing), both marked JEV. `system-message-router.ts` composes the noise classifier and is JEV for the same reason. None of the three is WRFC-controller/workstream/attempt-judging code itself; where they touch old WRFC vocabulary (a literal `"WRFC chain … transitioned"` match, a `wrfc()` delivery-kind method), that is a consumer of contract-chain lifecycle events, not the judging logic the DROP rule targets, so it carries over renamed to contract-runner terms rather than being dropped.

`src/test/core` surfaced ten files that do not test this product's own code at all: they import `Orchestrator`, `classifyIntent`, `prepareConversationForTurn`, `DeterministicReplayEngine`, `SessionLineageTracker`, `SessionMemoryStore`, `estimateConversationTokens`/`shouldAutoCompact`/`compactMessages`/`checkAndCompact`, `getAutoCompactDecision`, `buildSyntheticResult`/`detectUnresolvedToolCalls`, and `InfiniteBuffer` directly from `@pellux/goodvibes-sdk/platform/core` or `@pellux/goodvibes-terminal-shell`. These are tests of engine-owned modules that happen to live under this product's test tree, not tests of product code, so they are HOIST rather than PORT: the intent document's own engine "core" subsystem row names exactly this ground ("orchestrator loop, conversation manager, tool registry, compaction, intent classifier, planner, decomposition ... the loop becomes the contract runner; intent is a choice ... compaction keeps a fidelity check"). Two of the ten pin a genuine guessed decision (`classifyIntent`'s keyword ladder, and the orchestrator loop that becomes the contract runner); the other eight are deterministic engine plumbing (token arithmetic, replay diffing, lineage/memory CRUD) with no guesswork of their own, so they get no decision-points row. The most WRFC-saturated file in the whole 92-file slice is `orchestrator.test.ts`: every test in it constructs a `wrfcController: { listChains: () => [] }` service stub and some assert on `wrfc:*`-keyed event/follow-up data. None of that is WRFC-controller/attempt-judging logic itself, it is a generic dependency-injection seam and its test fixtures, so it is not DROP; it does mean the port work here is a full rewrite of that file's setup helpers to the contract runner's equivalent seam, not a light rename.

Files: 92. PORT 76, JEV 6, HOIST 10, DROP 0.

The `WRFC` entry in the banned-term list is vocabulary policy, not WRFC-controller code, so this stays PORT; the list's content (not its mechanism) will need a pass once the contract runner's own vocabulary is set. |
| `src/core/setup-incomplete-hint.ts` | PORT | `buildSetupIncompleteHint`/`pickNextStep` dispatch on already-typed `OnboardingState` phases/step statuses and do fixed string truncation. No guessing. |

It also contains one WRFC-specific sub-rule (a literal `"WRFC chain <id> transitioned … waiting for action"` match, suppressed via an injected terminal-state lookup); this is a consumer of WRFC replay state, not WRFC-controller/attempt-judging code itself, so per the owner ruling it carries over adapted to contract-tree/contract-chain terms rather than being dropped. See decision-points table. |
| `src/core/system-message-router.ts` | JEV | Composes `classifyNoise` (above) and carries a `wrfc()` delivery method labelling messages `SystemMessageKind: 'wrfc'`; the routing/tiering logic itself is deterministic dispatch on a closed target/priority enum, but it calls the judgment-call noise gate, and the `wrfc()` naming needs to become a contract-runner equivalent, a rename and a live delivery path, not a drop. |

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/config/settings-search-vocabulary.ts`, `settingDomainAliasText` / `SETTING_DOMAIN_VOCABULARY` | A hand-maintained per-domain alias word-list (e.g. "credit card", "spending limit", "card on file" for `payments`) that a settings search indexes beside the schema key/description, because the schema's own words miss what a person actually types | Jev choice/rerank reading over the closed set of settings domains, given the person's free-text search query, replacing the hand-authored alias list with a live judgment of which domain the query means |
| `src/core/activity-feed.ts`, `classifyActivityKind` | A 6-branch keyword/regex ladder over a message's bracket tag guessing one of 7 `ActivityKind` values | Jev choice reading over the closed `ActivityKind` set, given the tag text |
| `src/core/system-message-noise.ts`, `classifyNoise` (and its caller `src/core/system-message-router.ts`, pinned again by `src/test/core/system-message-noise.test.ts` and `system-message-router.test.ts`) | A hand-tuned regex ladder deciding emit/drop/fold "noise-worthiness" for operational status text, plus a WRFC-chain-specific terminal-state suppression rule keyed to literal `"WRFC chain … transitioned … waiting for action"` text | Jev choice reading (emit/drop/fold) scaled to low stakes for ambient feed traffic; the WRFC-chain sub-rule becomes a contract-chain equivalent read off the contract runner's own terminal-state signal, not a text match |
| `src/core/system-message-router.ts`'s `classifyPriority` (pinned by `src/test/core/system-message-router.test.ts`) | A bracket-tag/keyword ladder (`[Model]`, `[Session]`, `[Recovery]`, `fatal`, `crash` vs. `[Scan]`, `[MCP]`, `[Plugin]`, `[Tool]`) deciding high vs. low delivery priority | Jev choice reading (high/low priority tier) scaled to low stakes for ambient system messages |
| Engine's `classifyIntent` (`@pellux/goodvibes-sdk/platform/core`, pinned by `src/test/core/intent-classifier.test.ts` and `orchestrator-turn-helpers.test.ts`'s `prepareConversationForTurn`) | A keyword/regex signal ladder (question words, action verbs, multiple-deliverable separators, file-path/extension mentions, parallelism keywords, spec/plan-reference words, message length) scoring free text into chat/task/project with a confidence number | Jev choice reading over the closed `{chat, task, project}` set, per the intent doc's engine-table entry ("intent is a choice") |
| Engine's `Orchestrator` loop (`@pellux/goodvibes-sdk/platform/core`, pinned by `src/test/core/orchestrator.test.ts`, and the `wrfcController` stub repeated in `streaming.test.ts` and `tool-result-reconciliation.test.ts`) | Not a text-guessing heuristic itself, but the loop the intent doc names as becoming the contract runner; its test fixtures currently inject a `wrfcController: { listChains: () => [] }` service and `wrfc:*`-keyed follow-up/event data | Becomes the contract runner (JEV); the injected service seam renames from `wrfcController` to the contract runner's equivalent service, and event/follow-up keys move from `wrfc:*` to contract-tree/contract-chain vocabulary |

The other eight HOIST files in `src/test/core` (`context-compaction.test.ts`, `context-validation.test.ts`, `history.test.ts`, `replay-engine.test.ts`, `session-lineage.test.ts`, `session-memory.test.ts`, `tool-result-reconciliation.test.ts`) test engine modules that are deterministic (token/threshold arithmetic, exact ledger comparison, append-only CRUD, line-buffer storage) and carry no guessed decision point of their own, so they get no decision-points row; their disposition is HOIST purely because the code under test physically belongs in the engine, not because anything in them is judged.

## Audio, shell, permissions, trust and verification

This slice covers 83 files from goodvibes-agent: `src/audio`, `src/shell`, `src/permissions`, `src/trust`, `src/capabilities`, `src/verification`, and the matching tests under `src/test/audio`, `src/test/voice`, `src/test/shell`, `src/test/permissions`, `src/test/trust`, `src/test/capabilities`, `src/test/verification`. Every file was read in full (or, for the two largest data-list files, in full structure plus representative sampling of their repetitive entries).

Three groups emerge. **Audio, shell and verification** are pure mechanics: capture/playback subprocess wiring, terminal paint/focus/size bookkeeping, UI-opener composition, and build/release verification harnesses built entirely on file existence, HTTP status codes, JSON parseability and exact-string/regex token-shape checks. None of it guesses at natural-language meaning; every threshold that looks like a number (`vadThreshold`, `FAILURE_THRESHOLD`-style constants, coverage estimates) is a configured value or a documented, test-audited estimate, not a model call, so these files are **PORT**. **Permissions** (`approval-posture.ts`, `broker-approval.ts`, `prompt.ts`) render and mirror decisions the SDK's gate computes elsewhere (risk level, classification, sandbox escalation); nothing here does its own guessing, but per the intent document these three become views over the JEV-ified gate, so they are **JEV** with no local decision point of their own. **Capabilities and trust** are the deterministic-by-ruling modules: capability probes are exclusively file/directory/config-key presence checks and registered-tool/MCP-connection checks (never response-text sniffing or identity guessing), and the trust modules (outward approvals, the untrusted-content port and its re-export) are the ones the intent document names as staying deterministic. Both move into the engine (capabilities into engine discovery/capability registry, trust into engine security) as **HOIST**, not JEV.

No exec-risk/command-safety classifier, no wake-word confidence guesswork, no capability probe that infers a service's identity from response text, and no free-text-to-risk-tier policy tool were found anywhere in this slice; every place that looks like a threshold or a classification is either a configured constant consumed as-is, or a decision already made by SDK code outside this slice's 83 files.

Files: 83. PORT 60, JEV 9, HOIST 14, DROP 0. Decision points: 0.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `src/audio` | 11 | 11 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| `src/capabilities` | 8 | 0 | 0 | 8 | 0 |
| `src/permissions` | 3 | 0 | 3 | 0 | 0 |
| `src/shell` | 18 | 18 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

| `src/verification` | 5 | 5 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| `src/test/audio` + `src/test/voice` | 9 | 9 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| `src/test/capabilities` | 2 | 0 | 0 | 2 | 0 |
| `src/test/permissions` | 6 | 0 | 6 | 0 | 0 |
| `src/test/shell` | 12 | 12 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| `src/test/trust` | 1 | 0 | 0 | 1 | 0 |
| `src/test/verification` | 5 | 5 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

### File table

| File | Disposition | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `src/audio/activation-sound.ts` | PORT | Synthesises a fixed two-tone WAV chime and plays the configured `voice.wake.activationSound` kind (`chime`/`custom`/`none`) through the streaming player; every branch is a fixed lookup on a closed enum plus a file read, no guesswork. |
| `src/audio/capture.ts` | PORT | Spawns the recorder subprocess for wake-word capture and adapts it to the SDK's `AudioCaptureOpener` shape; the framing/policy logic it depends on lives in the SDK, this file only opens a process and pipes stdio. |
| `src/audio/input-devices.ts` | PORT | Lists PulseAudio sources via `pactl` and parses them with the SDK's `parsePactlSources`; a missing `pactl` throws (deliberately, to be read as "cannot tell" rather than "no microphones"), a deterministic distinction, not a guess. |
| `src/audio/onnxruntime-assets.d.ts` | PORT | Ambient module declarations for two onnxruntime-web asset imports (`with { type: 'file' }`). Pure type-level plumbing. |
| `src/audio/player.ts` | PORT | Resolves `mpv`/`ffplay` off PATH and streams audio bytes into its stdin with head/tail drain handling; PATH resolution and process lifecycle only, no content classification. |
| `src/audio/spoken-turn-model-routing.ts` | PORT | Swaps in a configured `tts.llmModel`/`tts.llmProvider` override only for turns tagged `source: 'tts'`, via an exact string/metadata-flag match (`isSpokenTurn`), not a guess. |
| `src/audio/spoken-turn-wiring.ts` | PORT | Wires the SDK's `SpokenTurnController` to this surface's runtime event bus; an `if (configManager.get('ui.voiceEnabled'))` toggle and event dispatch, nothing judged. |
| `src/audio/voice-capture-wiring.ts` | PORT | Composition root that wires the capture opener, STT gateway, activation sound and wake runtime together for this surface; assembles injected dependencies, decides nothing itself. |
| `src/audio/wake-inference.ts` | PORT | Extracts the two onnxruntime-web assets to disk (content-checked, not timestamp-checked) and adapts an ONNX session to the SDK's `WakeInferenceSession` shape; `numThreads = 1` is a documented engineering constant, not a tuned classification. |
| `src/audio/wake-runtime.ts` | PORT | Wires the SDK's `WakeListener`/`WakeWordEngine` to this surface's capture, transcription, activation-sound and diagnostics; the wake front end, thresholds (`vadThreshold`, tuning), patience/cooldown and restart policy all live in the SDK, this file only resolves settings and dispatches callbacks. |
| `src/audio/wake-surface.ts` | PORT | One exported constant (`AGENT_WAKE_SURFACE = 'agent'`), split into its own file purely to avoid a heavy import chain reaching the settings path. |
| `src/capabilities/builtin-capabilities.ts` | HOIST | Declares this build's capability list (browser control, email/calendar placeholders) entirely as file/config-presence prerequisites (`module-resolvable`, `config-value-present`, `any-file-present`); no response-text or identity guessing. Moves into the engine's capability registry per the intent document's "Capabilities: builtin capabilities... to the engine gate/discovery" bullet. |
| `src/capabilities/capability-boot-check.ts` | HOIST | Boot-time orchestrator that registers builtins, resolves the index, and logs any disagreement; every check underneath it is a read, never an effect. Same engine-capabilities disposition as its siblings. |
| `src/capabilities/capability-index.ts` | HOIST | The authoritative resolver: matches declarations against `ProbeContext` and computes ready/needs-setup/unavailable plus "configured but unreported" disagreements. All matching is over probe results (file/config/tool-registration booleans), never text content. |
| `src/capabilities/capability-probe-runner.ts` | HOIST | The single place a capability probe executes; every branch (`file-present`, `any-file-present`, `mcp-server-connected`, `module-resolvable`, etc.) is a filesystem stat, a JSON key check, or a set-membership test. Explicitly documented as read-only and effect-free by design, so it is the deterministic engine primitive the capability registry hoists onto, not a guessed classifier. |
| `src/capabilities/capability-snapshot.ts` | HOIST | Module-level cache of the resolved `CapabilityIndexReport` for the prompt composer to read synchronously; a get/set pair with a null-vs-empty distinction, no logic to judge. |
| `src/capabilities/capability-sources.ts` | HOIST | Adapters that turn live app state (config presence, MCP server posture, served operator-method ids) into probe inputs; every function fails closed on a try/catch, no interpretation of content. |
| `src/capabilities/capability-types.ts` | HOIST | Type/interface definitions only for the capability probe and declaration shapes; the header explicitly states probes are descriptions, not functions, so a registrant cannot smuggle an effect past the runner. |
| `src/capabilities/known-service-evidence.ts` | HOIST | A fixed, hand-maintained list of known credential file locations (e.g. `~/.gmail-mcp/credentials.json`) used only to detect "configured but unclaimed" capabilities; narrow known-location checks, never a disk scan or content read. |
| `src/permissions/approval-posture.ts` | JEV | Pure precedence computation mirroring the SDK gate's `checkDetailed()` order (autoApprove -> allow-all -> plan -> accept-edits -> custom -> prompt); computes today's posture deterministically from config, but per the intent document ("Permissions: approval posture... as views over the gate") this becomes a view over the JEV-ified gate rather than re-deriving precedence locally. No separate guessed classification found inside the file itself. |
| `src/permissions/broker-approval.ts` | JEV | Renders a broker-originated approval ask as an agent-terminal permission card and resolves it back through the shared `ApprovalBroker`; pure state wiring (status checks, deferred re-check), no judgment of its own, but is the agent's view over the gate/control-plane per the intent document. |
| `src/permissions/prompt.ts` | JEV | Renders the permission prompt UI from `PermissionRequestAnalysis` (risk level, classification, reasons) that the SDK gate already computed; its own `fallbackAnalysis` is a fixed switch (`category === 'write' ? 'medium' : ...`), a deterministic fallback, not a guess. Classified JEV because it is a view over the gate, per the intent document, not because it guesses anything itself. |

E.5 reading (2026-09-27 ruling): code, and not because the intent calls it deterministic: the approval is the owner's own answer to a prompt showing that exact call, and what code checks is that a later call carries the same content (hash equality), within its time limit, once; nothing about the call's meaning is decided there. All thresholds and tuning constants are configured values consumed as-is; the detection scoring itself is SDK code outside this slice, and no free-text classification appears in this test. PORT. |

### Decision points

None found in this slice. Every file was checked against the four flagged patterns (wake-word/voice confidence thresholds, exec-risk/command-safety classification, capability probing that infers a service's identity from response text, and a policy/posture tool that classifies free text into a risk tier):

- **Wake-word thresholds** (`vadThreshold`, detector/supervisor tuning) are configured numeric settings resolved and consumed as-is by `src/audio/wake-runtime.ts` and its SDK-owned engine; the classifier that scores audio frames lives entirely in the SDK (out of this slice), not in any of these 83 files.
- **Exec-risk/command-safety classification** does not appear in this slice at all; the permission gate's own risk analysis (`PermissionRequestAnalysis`) is computed in the SDK and only rendered by `src/permissions/prompt.ts`.
- **Capability probing** (`src/capabilities/*`) is exclusively file/directory/config-key presence and registered-tool/MCP-connection checks; nothing inspects a response body's text to infer what service produced it.
- **Policy/posture tools classifying free text** are not present in this slice; `src/permissions/*` renders decisions already made elsewhere.

Because no local guesswork exists in the 9 JEV files (`src/permissions/*` and its 6 tests) or the 14 HOIST files (`src/capabilities/*`, `src/trust/*` and their tests), their dispositions rest entirely on the intent document's own classification (permissions become "views over the gate"; capabilities and trust modules "stay deterministic" but move into the engine), not on a decision point discovered within this slice's files.

## CLI

Every file of this slice (79: `src/cli` and its matching tests under `src/test/cli`, plus `src/test/cli-flags.test.ts`), read in full, with its disposition and, for a JEV or HOIST file, its decision points. The intent document's own PORT bullet list for `products/agent`'s CLI names nearly every module in this slice by name (knowledge args/command/format/methods/runtime, browser, bundle, channel profiles, CI, completion, config overrides, connected host metrics, endpoints, entrypoint, external runtime, fleet, help, import, auto-update, local library, management, memory, OpenClaw import, operator args, owner profile, package verification, parser, personas, principals, profiles, provider auth routes, redaction, relay, resume notice, routines, run turn, service posture, skill bundles, skills, status, temporal label, TUI startup, workspaces), but naming a module PORT there is not a ruling that every line in it is free of a decision point; each file below was read on its own evidence. One file explicitly named for HOIST elsewhere in the intent document (`provider-classification.ts`, hoisting to the engine's `providers` subsystem alongside provider fallback and health) is the sole HOIST in this slice. Two files genuinely guess or judge, or carry a fixed enum that the platform is replacing with a Jev-scored equivalent, and are marked JEV: `agent-knowledge-runtime.ts` (a local substring-keyword error classifier) and `channel-profiles-command.ts` (the `--permission-mode` fixed enum, becoming the gate presets sheet). `fleet-command.ts` is the WRFC best-of-N attempt-judging surface named in this task's own instructions; per the owner ruling that such surfaces are never DROP, it becomes JEV, a view of the contract tree, with the actual judging living in the orchestration engine/contract runner rather than in this file. `help.ts` documents both the fleet judge/pick surface and the channel-profiles permission-mode enum in its usage/summary text, so it is marked JEV too, matching the webui inventory's own precedent that a file describing a JEV surface is itself JEV even when its own logic is plain string-building with no decision point of its own. Tests are marked to match the disposition of what they substantively exercise: `channel-profiles-command.test.ts` and `fleet-command.test.ts` follow their JEV source files; `provider-classification.test.ts` follows its HOIST source file; `help.test.ts` does not itself exercise the fleet or permission-mode help text and stays PORT.

Deterministic boundaries stayed code throughout, as instructed: `redaction.ts` (fixed regexes and a named-in-full credential-path set, a security boundary), `package-verification.ts` (a 4013-line deterministic build/release/text-manifest gate: pinned script strings, tarball path sets, exact tokenization of CLI-command mentions in package-facing docs against the parser's own closed command catalog, checksum/version/date arithmetic; no fuzzy or free-text guessing anywhere in it), `provider-auth-routes.ts`, `service-posture.ts` (structural network/bind-posture classification, not text guessing), `status.ts`'s security findings (auto-approve/allow-all/plaintext-secret checks are all exact equality on closed enum values), `temporal-label.ts` and `resume-relaunch-notice.ts` (date/duration arithmetic), and `completion.ts`/`parser.ts`/`help.ts`'s own dispatch logic (fixed alias tables and exact string equality, no fuzzy "did you mean" ranking or similarity scoring anywhere in this slice's command parsing, completion, or help generation). `openclaw-import.ts` is a pure structural file-shape scanner (fixed filenames/frontmatter keys/JSON shapes) that explicitly documents itself as never guessing. `agent-knowledge-command.ts`'s `handleDelegateCommand` sets a `wrfcRequested` metadata flag from a `--review`/`--wrfc` CLI flag and forwards it verbatim to a session-create call; the dispatch itself is a closed-set flag toggle (not a guess), so the file stays PORT, though the wire field name will need renaming to match the contract-runner vocabulary once WRFC is retired platform-wide.

Files: 79. PORT 71, JEV 6, HOIST 2, DROP 0. Decision points: 1.

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/cli/agent-knowledge-runtime.ts`, `classifyKnowledgeError` | A lower-cased substring-keyword ladder over a caught error's message text (`'401'`/`'unauthorized'`/`'auth'` -> `auth_required`; `'404'`/`'not found'` -> a further host-status probe; `'fetch'`/`'connect'`/`'econnrefused'` -> `connected_host_unavailable`; anything else -> a generic `connected_host_error`), the same shape of guess as the webui inventory's flagged `classifyBadgeTone`. | A Jev classify reading in the engine's routing/classify battery, taking the error text (and, where available, the real numeric status) and returning one of the same closed failure kinds; where the underlying call can be changed to preserve the actual HTTP status code instead of stringifying it into a message, that piece reverts to a deterministic status-code branch instead (see `connected-host-metrics.ts`/`external-runtime.ts` for the pattern this file should have used). |

Two further JEV files carry no decision-points row of their own, matching the webui inventory's precedent for WRFC-state views and for docs describing a JEV surface: `channel-profiles-command.ts` (the `--permission-mode` enum is a closed-set validation today; the judgment of which preset fits a given situation's stakes will live in the engine gate's Jev-scored stakes table, not in this CLI file) and `fleet-command.ts` (the best-of-N attempt judgment already lives in the orchestration engine's `proposeAttemptWinner`, outside this CLI file, which only renders the proposal and gates the confirm-required pick). `help.ts` documents both surfaces but performs no judgment of its own. `provider-classification.ts` (HOIST) is a deterministic fixed-set classification relocating to sit beside the engine's other provider-registry code; it does not become a Jev reading.

## Providers, types, and top-level (root, scripts, docs, release, packaging)

Every tracked file of goodvibes-agent under `src/providers`, `src/types`, `src/test/providers`, and every top-level file outside `src/` (94 files), read in full, with its disposition. This slice's own `src/providers` directory holds exactly one file (`reasoning-effort-surface.ts`); the provider registry, classification, fallback, and health logic the intent document expects to find there lives entirely in `@pellux/goodvibes-sdk` (a separate old repo, `packages/engine` in the new tree, already inventoried on its own), not in goodvibes-agent. This repo's own `src/providers` file and its 22 tests in `src/test/providers` only consume and assert against that imported engine behavior; they carry no classification, keyword ladder, or heuristic of their own. Nothing in this slice is WRFC- or QEMU-specific: no file references QEMU at all, and the one hit for "WRFC" (`src/test/providers/openai-compat.test.ts:77`) is arbitrary fixture prose inside a test message body, not WRFC-controller code. So this slice has zero DROP rows.

Six files are JEV, not because they contain guesswork of their own, but because they are product-facing docs that describe, in detail, Agent-product subsystems this port changes: the best-of-N `fleet.attempts.judge`/`.pick` surface (replaced by contract-runner candidate selection), the `agent_work_plan`/`agent_orchestration`/`delegation` runners (DROP, replaced by the contract runner, per the intent document's Agent-product DROP list), `agent_model_compare` blind-judgment tooling and the review-packet tools (DROP/JEV per intent), route-planner wording, permission-mode prediction, and hardware/route readiness scoring (the intent's "model comparison judgment is JEV, candidate selection replacing the prompt-and-parse judge" item). Two more, `.goodvibes/GOODVIBES.md` and `.goodvibes/agents/reviewer.md`, are this repo's own operating policy and reviewer-subagent brief, and both name WRFC explicitly as the current delegation/review model ("WRFC is never the default reasoning path", "no default WRFC") that changes once WRFC becomes the contract runner. None of these six files performs any classification itself (they are prose or a policy brief), so none earns a decision-points row, matching the pattern already established for `docs/architecture.md` and `README.md` in `docs/inventory/webui.md`.

Files: 94. PORT 88, JEV 6, HOIST 0, DROP 0. Decision points: 0.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| Top-level docs and screenshots | 15 | 11 | 4 | 0 | 0 |
| Release evidence | 5 | 5 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

| `src/test/providers` | 22 | 22 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

| `scripts/perf-check.ts` | PORT | Loads `release/performance-snapshot.json`, validates its shape field-by-field, evaluates it against `DEFAULT_BUDGETS` fixed numeric thresholds, and exits non-zero on violation. Deterministic budget comparison. |
| `scripts/post-build-smoke.ts` | PORT | Thin adapter to the shared toolchain's `runPostBuildSmoke`, sourcing the banner prefix and forbidden-string sentinels from `toolchain.config.json`. |
| `scripts/prebuild.ts` | PORT | Runs `patchBunCompileCompatibility` and `syncProjectSurfaces` under a workspace lock. Deterministic orchestration. |
| `scripts/project-surfaces.ts` | PORT | Syncs the exact-semver version from `package.json` into `src/version.ts`, the README badge, and `docs/README.md`'s release-line string via fixed regex replacement. Deterministic. |
| `scripts/publish-check.ts` | PORT | Runs the SDK release gates, release-metadata/package-facing-text checks, builds the package runtime, then inspects `npm pack --json` output for forbidden/required/oversized tarball entries by exact path/size comparison. |
| `scripts/publish-package.ts` | PORT | Stages a publish directory from the `package.json` `files` list (excluding forbidden paths), validates required docs/paths, and runs `npm publish` (or `pack --dry-run`). Deterministic staging and validation. |
| `scripts/release.ts` | PORT | Bumps the version, updates `CHANGELOG.md`/`src/version.ts`, checks release-evidence text hygiene (trailing whitespace, space-before-tab), and creates the git commit/tag. Its git-status filter (`releaseBlockingGitStatusLines`) is an exact path-membership/status-letter check, not guesswork. |
| `scripts/run-tests.ts` | PORT | Collects `*.test.ts`/`*.spec.ts` files under `src`, sweeps stale temp directories, and runs one `bun test` invocation with an isolated temp root. Deterministic. |
| `scripts/sdk-dev.ts` | PORT | Locates the sibling `goodvibes-sdk` checkout and forwards argv to its own `sdk-dev.ts`. No overlay logic of its own. |
| `scripts/sdk-release-gates.ts` | PORT | Thin adapter over the shared toolchain's `runSdkPinGate` (overlay-marker hard-fail, exact-pin/lock/installed agreement, npm-only import sweep). Deterministic gate results, mapped to issue strings. |
| `scripts/stage-browser-driver.ts` | PORT | Copies `node_modules/playwright-core` beside the compiled binary and asserts `index.js`/`cli.js` exist. Deterministic file staging. |
| `scripts/stale-tmp-sweep.ts` | PORT | Sweeps stale scratch entries in the real `os.tmpdir()` that match one of ~180 exact known prefixes AND are older than a fixed one-hour age gate; unconditionally empties the in-repo `.test-tmp/` root. Exact-prefix membership and mtime comparison, not a guessed classification. Some listed prefixes (e.g. `goodvibes-agent-work-plan-tool-`, `fleet-attention-db-`) correspond to test files for features the intent document drops elsewhere; the sweep script itself is unaffected either way since unmatched prefixes are simply harmless no-ops, so it is not itself dead tooling. |
| `scripts/verification-ledger.ts` | PORT | CLI wrapper printing `buildVerificationLedger`/`renderVerificationLedgerMarkdown` output as JSON or Markdown, optionally to a directory. Thin I/O wrapper. |
| `scripts/verify-live.ts` | PORT | CLI wrapper around `buildLiveVerificationReport`, parsing `--home`/`--binary`/`--connected-host-url`/`--strict` flags. Thin I/O wrapper. |
| `scripts/workspace-lock.ts` | PORT | Directory-based mutual-exclusion lock with a stale-lock clear based on process liveness (`process.kill(pid, 0)`) and age. Deterministic liveness check, not a guess. |

It builds user-facing wording (headline, per-level description, "Sent on the wire" line) from an already-resolved spec and labels a `fallback`-sourced spec with a fixed caveat sentence; the actual model-id-to-family resolution (the SDK's "curated family table" and "labelled best guess" precedence) lives in the engine, not here. Matches the intent document's explicit "Also the reasoning-effort surface" PORT line under the TUI's Permissions bullet (the same pattern applies to this product's own copy); only its import path changes to point at the engine. |
| `src/types/sql-js.d.ts` | PORT | Local ambient type declaration for `sql.js` (which ships no types); kept local rather than sourced from the SDK because an ambient declaration only takes effect for a compilation that includes the file. Pure typing, no logic. |

### `src/test/providers`

All 22 files import their subject classes/functions directly from `@pellux/goodvibes-sdk/platform/providers` (`ProviderRegistry`, `ProviderCapabilityRegistry`, `ProviderOptimizer`, `OpenAICompatProvider`, `LMStudioProvider`, `OpenAICodexProvider`, `FavoritesStore`, catalog/benchmark/discovery types, etc.), exercising engine-owned provider-registry, catalog, and adapter behavior already accounted for under the engine's own `providers` subsystem row (disposition PORT there, per the intent document: "PORT; hoists provider classification, fallback, health, and model comparison and routing" describes logic moving INTO that subsystem from elsewhere, not the subsystem itself becoming judged). None of these test files contains a classifier, keyword ladder, or scoring heuristic of its own; every one asserts an exact, deterministic expected outcome (prefix/substring matching rules, fixed fallback-caveat text, exact JSON-shape assertions). They carry over with only their import paths changing to point at `packages/engine`.

### Decision points

None. Every JEV file in this slice is a document or policy brief describing a subsystem this port changes elsewhere (the contract runner, the gate's permission-mode presets, the engine's model-comparison/routing batteries); none of the six performs a guessed classification of its own that would earn a "Where / Guessed today / Replaced by" row. The one file that discusses its own fallback logic in code, `src/providers/reasoning-effort-surface.ts`, delegates the actual model-id-to-family resolution to the engine (`@pellux/goodvibes-sdk/platform/providers`), so the decision point, if any, belongs to that engine subsystem's own inventory, not to this file.

## Runtime (bootstrap, composition, spine transports)

The alphabetical first half of `src/runtime` (through `tool-permission-safety.ts`, `client/*`, `store/*`, `diagnostics/panels/*`, `onboarding/*`) plus its matching tests under `src/test/runtime`, 107 files, read in full. This slice is overwhelmingly the agent's composition root: bootstrap phases, the client seams that reach the daemon (approvals, fleet union, hosted-session frames, session inputs), the runtime store/selectors, onboarding, and a run of small named subsystems the intent document lists verbatim under the agent's Runtime PORT bullet (browser driver profile, calendar boot refresh, client/build compatibility, connected host auth, context, conversation rewind port, daemon build compatibility/CLI service/receipts/repair, feature enablement, LAN scan consent, MCP lazy start and suggestion scan, occasions boot and nudge surface, onboarding, operator token cleanup, path shadow, periodic update, process fault capture, provider account snapshot, provider boot, release artifacts, rewind anchors, self-update receipt, services, store, tar archive, UI read models and services, the unhandled rejection guard and update check). All of that carries over as PORT: every one of these modules is either explicitly named in the intent document's PORT list or is deterministic wiring/parsing/version-comparison code with no natural-language guesswork (fixed platform/arch name tables, tar header byte-flag parsing, exit-code contracts, JSON schema validation, count/threshold engineering constants).

Four files carry a real decision point and are marked accordingly. `execution-ledger.ts` and `tool-permission-safety.ts` are exactly the "execution ledger" and "tool permission safety" modules the intent document names as HOIST targets ("The exec posture, operator policy, execution ledger and tool permission safety go to the gate and contract runner"), and both contain genuine keyword/name-ladder classifications that become Jev readings. `agent-exec-posture.ts` and `agent-operator-policy.ts` HOIST for the same textual reason but carry no local guesswork of their own (a deterministic tmux-name-prefix rule and a natural-language system-prompt block, respectively). `memory-spine-rest-transport.ts` and `session-spine-rest-transport.ts` HOIST as "the spine transports"; `memory-spine-adoption.ts` and `memory-usage-wiring.ts` HOIST as "memory usage wiring" (the latter also feeds an SDK-side heuristic overlap detector, noted as a decision point). One test file, `compaction/quality-score.test.ts`, pins a heuristic (compression ratio plus keyword-based "handoff" detection against a hand-tuned `LOW_QUALITY_THRESHOLD`) that the intent document already calls out by name ("compaction quality uses the fidelity pattern"), so it is marked JEV.

Files: 107. PORT 98, JEV 1, HOIST 8, DROP 0. Decision points: 4.

| File | Disposition | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `src/runtime/agent-browser.ts` | PORT | Binds the SDK's surface-agnostic `BrowserEngine`/`BrowserSessionManager` to this product's four answers (profile root, screenshot root, driver search dirs, untrusted-content port). Explicitly named "the agent browser" in the intent document's Runtime PORT list. No guesswork; the untrusted-content port and session-write ledger wiring are deterministic plumbing. |
| `src/runtime/agent-conversation-sender.ts` | PORT | Registers this product as the `agent` channel-delivery destination; resolves an addressed conversation by exact id lookup and throws (never silently falls back) when unresolved. Explicitly named "conversation sender" in the intent's PORT list. No guessed classification. |
| `src/runtime/agent-conversational-capture.ts` | PORT | Composes the SDK's `resolveCaptureAuthority`/`CONVERSATIONAL_TURN_TOOLS` with this product's own tool list and a restated (not re-derived) instruction policy. Explicitly named "conversational capture" in the intent's PORT list. The policy text is natural-language instruction to the model, not a coded guess. |
| `src/runtime/agent-exec-posture.ts` | HOIST | Matches the intent's HOIST bullet "The exec posture, operator policy, execution ledger and tool permission safety go to the gate and contract runner." States `AGENT_OWNER_TERMINAL_GUARD = { posture: 'enforced' }`: the exec tool refuses to type into a tmux session unless its name carries the platform's own `goodvibes-` prefix. Now in the engine at `packages/engine/sdk/src/platform/gate/policy/exec-posture.ts`. E.5 reading (2026-09-27 ruling): kept as code because the platform names the sessions it creates, so the prefix is the platform recognising its own sessions by the name it gave them; no reading of the command or session content is involved, and nothing about the owner's session can be judged from text Jev would see. |
| `src/runtime/agent-operator-policy.ts` | HOIST | The same "operator policy" named in the exec-posture/execution-ledger/tool-permission-safety HOIST bullet. Its content is a natural-language system-prompt block (`GOODVIBES_AGENT_OPERATOR_POLICY`) read by the model each turn, not a coded classifier, so it hoists with no local decision-point code of its own. Now in the engine at `packages/engine/sdk/src/platform/gate/policy/operator-policy.ts` (`GOODVIBES_AGENT_OPERATOR_POLICY_LINES`, `buildAgentOperatorPolicy(capturePolicy)`; the capture contract stays with the agent and is passed in). |

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/runtime/execution-ledger.ts`, `routeKindForTool` | A seven-way keyword ladder over the tool name and `args.command` (browser/desktop/screenshot -> `browser`; delegate/agent -> `delegation`; exec/shell/bash or a string `command` arg -> `shell`; write/edit/patch/delete -> `write`; fetch/web/http/url -> `network`; read/find/inspect/grep/search -> `read`; else `other`) decides the execution ledger's displayed route-kind, with an explicit documented precedence order (shell beats write/read) to patch a misclassification. | A Jev choice reading (a side-effect-kind battery over the tool call), in the engine gate, per the intent document's gate row ("side-effect, risk-family and sandbox-advisory batteries"). Done in E.5: `readSideEffectKind` (gate/reading.ts) in `packages/engine/sdk/src/platform/gate/policy/execution-ledger.ts`. |
| `src/runtime/tool-permission-safety.ts`, `fallbackPermissionCategory` / `fallbackPermissionCategoryForArgs` | When the real `PermissionManager` throws, roughly twenty hardcoded name/action-string sets (`READ_TOOL_NAMES`, `WRITE_TOOL_NAMES`, `EXECUTE_TOOL_NAMES`, and per-tool `READ_ONLY_*_ACTIONS` keyword sets covering `process`, `schedule`, `settings`, `setup`, `vibe`, `personal_ops`, `research`, `channels`, `memory`, `browser`, `computer`, `device`, `models`, `workspace`, `autonomy`, `delegation`, `execution`, `agent_harness`, `agent_artifacts`, `agent_review_packet_presets`, plus a normalized-action-vocabulary split for `profile`/`occasions`) classify a tool call into read/write/execute/delegate by matching the action string against each list. | A Jev side-effect-kind classification (choice reading) as the gate's fallback path, consistent with the intent document's gate row and its naming of this exact module for HOIST. Done in E.5: `categoryForSideEffectKind(readSideEffectKind(...).kind)` in `packages/engine/sdk/src/platform/gate/policy/tool-permission-safety.ts`; no table kept. |
| `src/runtime/memory-usage-wiring.ts`, `MemoryUsageTracker.onTurnCompleted` (via the SDK's `detectReferencedMemoryIds`) | An "honest heuristic overlap" (the module's own description) between injected-memory summaries/details and the model's turn output text decides which memories are credited as used, feeding the consolidation decay ordering. | A Jev existence-check/fidelity reading on retrieval, per the engine's knowledge-domain row ("rerank and existence check on retrieval"). |
| `src/test/runtime/compaction/quality-score.test.ts` (pinning the SDK's `computeQualityScore`/`escalateStrategy`) | A hand-tuned score blending a compression-ratio formula with a fixed-string "handoff" keyword check (looking for session-continuity phrasing in the compacted output) and a magic `LOW_QUALITY_THRESHOLD`/letter-grade cutoff decide whether a compaction pass is "low quality" and whether to escalate strategy (microcompact -> autocompact -> collapse). | The engine's fidelity pattern, per the intent document's own note that "compaction quality uses the fidelity pattern." |

The other alphabetical half of `goodvibes-agent`'s `src/runtime` tree, covered here entirely through its tests under `src/test/runtime` (107 files, all test files; the paired source files are covered by the sibling slice runtime-a). Each file was read in full. Per the owner ruling of 2026-09-26, nothing is dropped unless it is specific to WRFC or QEMU; every other module is PORT, JEV (its guessed decision point becomes a Jev reading) or HOIST (moves into `packages/engine`, naming the specific subsystem from the intent document's table). Exactly one file in this slice is WRFC-controller specific and gets DROP; nothing here is QEMU-specific. Most of this slice is deterministic bootstrap, transport, store and settings-registration wiring, explicitly named PORT in the intent document's agent Runtime bullet list. The gate cluster (exec-posture/command-risk classification, policy bundle lifecycle, permission-manager fallback categories, MCP tool-call coherence and schema quarantine) HOISTs to the engine gate, per the intent document's HOIST bullets naming "exec posture, operator policy, execution ledger and tool permission safety" and "every side effect goes through the gate." The memory/session spine transports and memory usage wiring HOIST to engine state, per the explicit HOIST bullet "the spine transports and memory usage wiring."

Deterministic config-key checks, no guessed classification. |

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `agent-platform-boundary-policy.ts`'s `ownerAskedAboutPlatformSource`, pinned by `conversational-capture-contract.test.ts` | A keyword/phrase ladder over the last user message (path mentions, "show me", "go fix", "look at" phrasing) deciding whether the owner authorized this turn to touch platform source. | A Jev yes/no reading on whether the turn's own words authorized reading/editing platform source, replacing the phrase ladder. |
| `classifyFailure`'s message-based branches, pinned by `forensics/forensics.test.ts` | Substring match on the turn/task error text ("timeout" -> `turn_timeout`; "rate limit"/"overloaded"/"503" -> `llm_error`) when no structural signal (stopReason, boolean flag) already resolved the class. | A Jev choice reading over the error text into the fixed failure-class vocabulary, used only when no deterministic signal already answers it. |
| `McpSchemaFreshnessTracker`'s auto-quarantine trigger, pinned by `mcp/schema-quarantine.test.ts` | A hard-coded consecutive-failure-count constant that flips a server's schema straight to `quarantined` (blocking every tool call on it) with no severity or context weighing. | A Jev score/threshold reading (or a reviewed battery) judging whether the failure pattern actually warrants blocking the server, not a bare consecutive-count trip wire. |
| `DivergenceDashboard.checkEnforceGate`, pinned by `permissions/divergence-dashboard.test.ts` and `permissions/policy-registry.test.ts` | A single hard-coded divergence-rate threshold (default 5%) gating whether a candidate policy bundle may be promoted to `enforce` mode, the same number regardless of what kind of rule diverged. | A Jev score/threshold reading scaled to the stakes of what diverged (per the intent document's "bands scaled to stakes"), replacing the flat percentage. E.5 ruling: stays code; it compares a counted ratio with the owner's configured threshold (arithmetic against an owner setting). |
| Exec-guard obfuscation detector (`evaluateCommandAST`/`evaluateSegmentNode`), pinned by `permissions/exec-guard-format-strings.test.ts` and `permissions/normalization/verdict.test.ts` | A regex ladder over command-segment text flagging base64, URL-encoding, null-byte, command-substitution and dangerous-context variable-expansion as "obfuscation." | A Jev yes/no reading on whether a command segment is genuinely obfuscated/evasive, in place of the pattern ladder (the catastrophic-command list and other deterministic boundaries stay code, per the intent document). |
| Command risk classification (`DEFAULT_POLICIES`), pinned by `permissions/normalization/verdict.test.ts` | A fixed keyword/command-name rule table sorting each command segment into `read`/`write`/`network`/`destructive`/`escalation`. | A Jev risk-family battery classifying command risk tier, as named in the intent document's gate row ("side-effect, risk-family and sandbox-advisory batteries"). |

Files: 107. PORT 81, JEV 3, HOIST 23 (4 of which also carry a JEV decision point), DROP 0. Decision points: 5.

## Agent surface (registries, gateways, channels, capabilities)

All 126 tracked files of `src/agent/**` (including `calendar/` and `signup/`) and their matching tests
under `src/test/agent/**`, read in full. This slice is the densest concentration of real decision
points in the port: skill-draft screening, setup-receipt inference, connected-host error
classification (three duplicated copies of the same keyword ladder), per-turn memory relevance
banding, and research-source credibility scoring. Nothing here is WRFC- or QEMU-specific, so this
slice has zero DROP rows. Files: 126. PORT 3, JEV 26, HOIST 97, DROP 0. Decision points: 11.

Several files carry explicit deterministic-by-design headers (`surface-authority.ts`,
`untrusted-content.ts`, `memory-safety.ts`, `payments-channel-guard.ts`) matching the intent
document's own instruction that these trust modules "stay deterministic" even though they HOIST;
they get no decision-points row. Two registry-adjacent "discovery" modules (`persona-discovery.ts`,
`routine-discovery.ts`, `skill-discovery.ts`) are deterministic filesystem scans that feed the JEV
registries but contain no guesswork of their own, so they are marked HOIST rather than JEV,
consistent with the intent document scoping "JEV" to "the persona, routine, note, document,
research-run and research-source **registries**" rather than to their discovery scanners.

Three genuinely duplicated keyword-ladders classify an opaque connected-host error string
(`'401'`, `'unauthorized'`, `'auth'`, `'404'`, `'not found'`, `'fetch'`, `'connect'`,
`'econnrefused'`) into one of four failure kinds, in `operator-gateway-call.ts`,
`routine-schedule-promotion.ts` and `routine-schedule-receipts.ts` independently. All three are the
same decision point and are consolidated into one row below with all three locations named.

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/agent/harness-control.ts` (`settingRelevance`, ~lines 251-263) | Hand-tuned weighted scoring ladder ranking a settings-catalog entry against a free-text query: exact key-text substring match +5000, +1000 per matched token in key text, +100 per matched token in description, +10 per matched token in domain-alias text, then sorted descending. Feeds `resolveHarnessSetting`'s ambiguous-vs-resolved decision. | A rerank reading over the settings catalog, replacing the fixed point-weight ladder with a judged relevance ranking. |
| `src/agent/operator-gateway-call.ts` (`classifyOperatorGatewayError`, ~lines 64-93); duplicated in `src/agent/routine-schedule-promotion.ts` (`classifyConnectedHostScheduleError`, ~lines 359-381) and `src/agent/routine-schedule-receipts.ts` (`classifyScheduleListError`, ~lines 664-690) | Three independent copies of the same substring-keyword ladder over a lower-cased, opaque thrown-error message: `'401'`/`'unauthorized'`/`'auth'` → `auth_required`; `'404'`/`'not found'` → probe `/status` then `connected_host_incompatible` or `connected_host_route_unavailable`; `'fetch'`/`'connect'`/`'econnrefused'` → `connected_host_unavailable`; else → `connected_host_error`. | A single classify/existence-check reading over the thrown error text, replacing all three keyword ladders with one judged failure-kind classification (still gated by the deterministic `/status` probe for the 404 case). |
| `src/agent/memory-prompt.ts` (`relevanceBand`, lines 83-87) | Fixed numeric thresholds banding a continuous 0-100 semantic-relevance percent into three qualitative labels: `>=60` "high match", `>=35` "moderate match", else "low match". | A score reading with bands scaled to stakes, replacing the two hardcoded cut points. |
| `src/agent/research-source-registry.ts` (`create()`, score default, line ~308) | Fixed credibility-to-score ladder used only when no explicit score is given: `credibility === 'high'` → 80, `'medium'` → 60, else → 50. | A score reading deriving the source's numeric credibility score directly, replacing the three-rung fixed default table (matches the intent's explicit "research-source credibility scored"). |
| `src/agent/skill-draft-proposer.ts` (`proposeSkillDrafts`, lines 26, 136-200) | Screens learning-curator candidates before auto-creating a disabled skill draft: fixed eligibility filter (`status` must be `'proposal-ready'` or `'ready-to-promote'` and `proposalTarget === 'skill'`), a hardcoded cap of 3 proposals per pass, and slug-based name dedup against existing/previously-proposed/within-pass names. | A policy-checklist reading screening the candidate list before promotion, replacing the fixed status-enum-plus-cap gate (matches the intent's explicit "drafts screened by policy checklist"). |
| `src/agent/setup-wizard-artifact-receipts.ts` (`inferStepIdFromMetadata`, lines 144-165) | Regex/keyword matching over a joined string of `receiptKind`/`methodId`/`purpose`/`source`/`label`/`summary`/filename fields to guess which of four setup steps (`browser-pwa`, `install-smoke`, `connected-host-auth`, `connected-host-readiness`) an artifact receipt with no explicit step id belongs to. | A classify/choice reading assigning the receipt to its setup step, replacing the four-pattern regex ladder. |
| `src/agent/setup-wizard-artifact-receipts.ts` (`normalizeStatus`, lines 87-121) | A ~20-entry keyword-list-to-enum mapping normalizing an arbitrary status string from mixed sources (`ready`, `ready-for-user-run`, `complete`, `ok`, `success`, `reachable`, `authenticated`, `published`, `usable`, `active`, `running`, `online`, `healthy` → `ready`; `blocked`, `not-ready`, `setup-needed`, `missing`, `unavailable`, `unreachable`, `unauthenticated`, `pending` → `blocked`; `failed`/`failure`/`error`/`errored` → `failed`; else → `unknown`). | A classify reading into the closed `{ready, blocked, failed, unknown}` set, the same coarsen/choice pattern as the webui `classifyBadgeTone` precedent, replacing the ~20-token keyword ladder. |
| SDK `detectReferencedMemoryIds`, pinned by `src/test/agent/memory-usage-detection.test.ts` | A guessed heuristic requiring "two distinctive tokens or one long distinctive token" (token length threshold, e.g. `>=6` chars) overlapping between generated output and a memory's summary/detail text, plus adjacent-phrase detection, to decide whether output "referenced" versus merely was "present" alongside a memory. Feeds the learning-curator's usefulness signal. | An existence-check / reply-reading pattern judging whether the output actually drew on the memory, replacing the token-length-and-overlap-count heuristic. |
| SDK `MemoryUsageStatsStore.summary()`, pinned by `src/test/agent/memory-usage-stats.test.ts` | Injected/referenced-count tallies used as a consolidation "usefulness" signal; the store's own `signalNote` field is self-labelled "heuristic" in its committed test assertion. | A score reading for memory usefulness, replacing the raw injected/referenced count ratio the store's own note admits is a heuristic (matches the intent's "usefulness as a score" for the learning curator). |

## Input: agent-workspace editors (contract-runner tools)

The intent document's DROP line for products/agent ("The delegation, agent orchestration, work plan, workspace action and editor runners, the workspace tool and the review packet tools") is void per the owner ruling of 2026-09-26: nothing is dropped unless it is specific to WRFC or QEMU. Every file in this slice was read in full. None of the ~90 `src/input/agent-workspace*.ts` editor/panel files, and none of the 13 matching workspace tests, are WRFC-controller-specific or QEMU-sandbox-specific code: they are ordinary in-modal form plumbing (field specs, confirm-gated slash-command string building, runtime-snapshot assembly, navigation, search, token routing) that dispatches to slash commands and first-class tools owned elsewhere. Per the intent document's own PORT section for this product ("The workspace's review-packet, work-plan and delegation editors submit contract-runner verbs"), the delegation editor, the work-plan editor, and the review-packet editor family carry over as JEV: they now submit contract-runner verbs instead of the retired WRFC verbs they targeted before. Everything else in this slice is plain PORT: the target of the command string changes only for those five files (plus the one test file that exercises the delegation editor's retired WRFC flag); no other file's behavior, interface, or target verb changes.

No file in this slice does any of its own free-text guessing (keyword ladders, regex classification, or score/verdict extraction from prose). The one WRFC-shaped artifact found is a literal `review`/`wrfc` confirm-field alias in the delegation editor that adds a `--review` flag to the dispatched `/delegate` command, requesting the old write-review-fix-confirm loop explicitly; that is the slice's one decision point. The "review packet" editors (document-ops-editor.ts, review-packet-snapshot.ts, review-packet-utils.ts) are deterministic bookkeeping over already-computed artifact metadata (model-compare judgments, handoffs, archives, route decisions); they hold no guesswork of their own to replace, the same pattern the webui inventory uses for contract-tree/judgment-view files that are JEV by virtue of the surface they render rather than by a local guessed classification.

Files: 110. PORT 104, JEV 6, HOIST 0, DROP 0. Decision points: 1.

| Area | Files | PORT | JEV | HOIST | DROP | Decision points |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Editor/command-editor framework, categories, settings, snapshot, navigation, search, token routing | 90 | 90 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| Delegation, work-plan, review-packet editors | 5 | 0 | 5 | 0 | 0 | 1 |
| Main `agent-workspace.ts` orchestrator | 1 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| Tests | 14 | 13 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

| `src/test/input/agent-workspace-command-editor-engine.test.ts` | PORT | Spec-pin regression suite for the twelve command-editor domains consolidated onto the shared engine (exact field defaults and dispatched command strings per kind). Deterministic. |
| `src/test/input/agent-workspace-command-parity.test.ts` | PORT | Cross-checks every workspace action against the registered command set, real categories, and concrete editors, plus CLI-command-to-workspace-coverage parity. Structural checks, no content classification. |
| `src/test/input/agent-workspace-connect-cards.test.ts` | PORT | Regression test that the inbox/calendar workspace cards are real dispatchable editors, not dead `kind:'guidance'` placeholders. Deterministic assertions. |
| `src/test/input/agent-workspace-editor-routing.test.ts` | PORT | Routing-exhaustiveness regression tests (command-editor kind coverage, local-registry unhandled-kind error surfacing, settingKey-to-CONFIG_SCHEMA exhaustiveness). Deterministic. |
| `src/test/input/agent-workspace-email-connect-editor.test.ts` | PORT | End-to-end test of the email-connect wizard against a real `ConfigManager` and in-memory secrets manager: honest error/success states, no raw-password leakage. Deterministic. |
| `src/test/input/agent-workspace-google-cards.test.ts` | PORT | Parity test that every `/google` subcommand has a matching workspace card (except the deliberately card-less `approve` route, asserted to stay card-less since it clears an outward-effect refusal). Deterministic. |
| `src/test/input/agent-workspace-onboarding-state.test.ts` | PORT | Unit and integration tests for onboarding reveal-set monotonicity, resume-entry derivation, and the `AgentWorkspace` ONBOARDING-mode sequencing/recap flow. Deterministic. |
| `src/test/input/agent-workspace-owner-profile-card.test.ts` | PORT | End-to-end tests of the Your Profile card family: read/get/provenance/status dispatch, the person-lookup `namedBy` gate, correction/forget confirmation gating. Deterministic. |
| `src/test/input/agent-workspace.test.ts` | JEV | The full `AgentWorkspace` integration test suite (4663 lines). Overwhelmingly deterministic UI-wiring regression tests, but two tests ("delegates build work from a confirmed workspace form without default WRFC" and "delegation workspace requests WRFC only when explicitly selected") directly assert the `/delegate` and `/delegate --review ...` command shapes tied to the delegation editor's WRFC-named checkbox (see `agent-workspace-delegation-editor-submission.ts` above); these two tests need to change in lockstep with that file once the checkbox and its `--review` flag are retired for the contract runner's always-on Jev checking. |
| `src/test/input/mcp-workspace-policy.test.ts` | PORT | Tests confirmation-gating for MCP add/remove/reload on the separate `McpWorkspace` (not `AgentWorkspace`) modal. Deterministic. |

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `agent-workspace-delegation-editor-submission.ts`, the `delegate-task` editor's `review`/`wrfc` confirm field (`buildAgentWorkspaceDelegationEditorSubmission`) | Not a free-text guess but a literal, user-facing checkbox named after WRFC: checking it appends `--review` to the dispatched `/delegate` command, explicitly requesting the retired write-review-fix-confirm loop on the delegated task. | The contract runner's real-time Jev nudging is now the default and only judging path for every delegated unit (sub-agents kept, per the project's standing rule); the `review`/`wrfc` checkbox and its `--review` flag are retired, since there is no separate heavyweight review pass left to opt into. `agent-workspace.test.ts`'s two WRFC-flag tests must be rewritten to match. |

## Input: commands, handler routes and other input modules

This fragment covers the first alphabetical half of `goodvibes-agent`'s `src/input` tree (101 files): the top-level input handler modules (`handler*.ts`, `feed-context-factory.ts`, `keybindings.ts`, `input-history.ts`, `search.ts`, `selection-modal.ts`, the model-picker family, the session/profile-picker modals, `mcp-workspace.ts`, `concealed-input.ts`/`plain-line-input.ts`, `file-picker.ts`, `command-registry.ts`, `autocomplete.ts`, `command-args-hint.ts`, `commands.ts`) plus the alphabetically-earlier half of `src/input/commands/*` (from `agent-local-library-args.ts` through `vibe-runtime.ts`). The roughly 90 agent-workspace files and their tests are a separate slice and are excluded here, per the intent document's Input bullet under products/agent.

Nearly everything in this slice is plain command-surface plumbing over already-judged or already-deterministic engine APIs (provider registry, knowledge/memory API, MCP API, secrets manager, session manager): it parses args, calls a typed SDK client, and prints a formatted report. Two genuine decision points were found, both matching the pattern the project is watching for: a closed-set command-dispatch fuzzy-ranker, and a vendor-family-guessing regex ladder directly analogous to `goodvibes-webui`'s flagged `detectFamily`. A third file (`delegation-runtime.ts`) is marked JEV purely for retired WRFC vocabulary in a wire field, with no guessed decision point of its own. Several other fuzzy-search-shaped functions were inspected and found NOT to be decision points (file-picker and selection-modal typeahead, `SearchManager`'s literal substring find, `model-picker-local-fit.ts`'s hardware-byte arithmetic): each is either a plain list filter the user still browses and explicitly picks from, or deterministic arithmetic, not a guess about what to do. No WRFC-controller/workstream/attempt-judging code and no QEMU-sandbox code exists in this slice, so there are no DROP rows.

Files: 101. PORT 98, JEV 3, HOIST 0, DROP 0. Decision points: 2.

#### File table

| File | Disposition | Note |
|---|---|---|

| `src/input/autocomplete.ts` | PORT | `AutocompleteEngine` is pure state management (active/query/results/selectedIndex) that delegates ranking to `CommandRegistry.fuzzyMatch`; it has no matching algorithm of its own, so the decision point lives in command-registry.ts, not here. |

| `src/input/command-registry.ts` | JEV | `scoreMatch` (used by `fuzzyMatch`) ranks slash commands by a hand-rolled prefix/subsequence heuristic over the CLOSED set of registered commands, exactly the "dispatch for closed-set commands" pattern the intent document names under engine routing. See decision-points table. |
