| `src/input/keybindings.ts` | PORT | `KeybindingsManager`; `comboMatches` is exact key/ctrl/shift/alt equality, and the inverted lookup map is a deterministic O(1) hash lookup. Fixed key bindings are explicitly a "never judged" category per project rule. |
| `src/input/mcp-workspace.ts` | PORT | Fullscreen MCP server browse/add/edit/delete/reload workspace; form validation (`validateServerName`, env `KEY=VALUE` parsing) is fixed-syntax parsing, and trust-mode options explicitly exclude `allow-all` from this surface (routed to Settings instead), a deterministic security boundary. |
| `src/input/model-picker-effort.ts` | PORT | `resolveEffortPickerState`/`buildEffortPickerItems` resolve a model's real offered reasoning-effort levels through the shared `effortPresentationForModel` surface; falls back to a shared static description table only for a level `details` does not cover. Deterministic capability lookup, not a guess. |
| `src/input/model-picker-local-fit.ts` | PORT | `buildLocalFitRecommendations` computes `fitAssessment(estimateModelBytes(params), profile)`, a numeric byte-size-vs-hardware comparison (RAM/disk arithmetic), not a guess from free text or vendor names; it is deterministic arithmetic the project explicitly keeps as code. `isProviderSignInRow`/`isLocalFitRecommendation` are exact sentinel-id/description-substring checks used only to route to the provider picker, never to commit a model silently. |
| `src/input/model-picker-types.ts` | JEV | `detectFamily`'s `FAMILY_PATTERNS` is a twelve-entry regex ladder (`/claude/i`, `/gpt\|\bo1\b.../i`, `/gemini/i`, `/llama/i`, `/qwen/i`, `/glm\|chatglm/i`, `/minimax\|abab/i`, `/deepseek/i`, `/mistral\|mixtral/i`, `/command\|cohere/i`, `/grok/i`, `/kimi\|moonshot/i`) guessing a model's vendor family from its id/displayName text, used by the picker's `groupBy: 'family'` display grouping. This is the same shape `docs/inventory/webui.md` flagged for its own `detectFamily` (a display-grouping convenience, not itself a routing/tier decision) and carries the identical tension with the project's "no vendor or model names in routing or tier rules": since this instance is purely a UI grouping filter rather than a routing or tier decision, the same owner ruling that resolves webui's flagged case should also resolve this one; if the owner reads the no-vendor-names rule as unconditional, the grouping feature should be dropped rather than given a Jev reading. See decision-points table. |
| `src/input/model-picker.ts` | PORT | `ModelPickerModal`; capability/category/benchmark-sort filters are boolean/enum checks over already-typed `ModelDefinition` fields, and `_getSyntheticSubgroup`'s threshold (`score >= A_TIER_THRESHOLD`) sorts on an already-computed composite score from the engine's model-comparison surface (inventoried elsewhere as JEV/HOIST). This file only renders and sorts precomputed values; it does not itself classify free text (aside from re-exporting `detectFamily` from model-picker-types.ts, covered there). |
| `src/input/panel-paste-flood-guard.ts` | PORT | `trackPanelPasteFloodGuard` is a deterministic sliding-window rate guard (fixed threshold/window constants) distinguishing an unbracketed-paste replay from real typing by wall-clock timing, explicitly documented as byte-identical to the TUI's port of the same module; a timing rule, not a content guess. |
| `src/input/payments-config.ts` | PORT | Synthetic config-key constants for the four card-material fields plus the seven address-field suffixes; pure data/constant declarations, all four secret keys route through the existing `SECRET_CONFIG_KEYS` masking mechanism (deterministic, security-adjacent, stays code). |
| `src/input/plain-line-input.ts` | PORT | The unmasked sibling of concealed-input.ts for address-field entry; same mechanical begin/submit/cancel shape, deliberately a separate type rather than a `conceal` boolean flag so no future refactor can accidentally echo a masked field. Deterministic composer plumbing. |
| `src/input/profile-picker-modal.ts` | PORT | `ProfilePickerModal`; `applyProfileCategory` copies only a hardcoded allow-list of `ConfigKey`s (`DISPLAY_KEYS`, `BEHAVIOR_KEYS`) from a loaded profile, and deletion/saving are explicitly disabled in Agent (redirected to Agent Workspace profiles) with a fixed message, a deterministic capability boundary. |
| `src/input/search.ts` | PORT | `SearchManager`'s `buildMatches`/`countOccurrences` do literal (lower-cased) substring search across the rendered transcript and collapsed blocks/turns, honestly counting hidden hits before they are revealed. This is a find-in-document feature (like a browser's Ctrl+F), not a fuzzy or guessed classification. |
| `src/input/selection-modal.ts` | PORT | `SelectionModal.fuzzyMatch` (substring-first, then char-by-char subsequence) ranks arbitrary caller-supplied items (sessions, bookmarks, templates) for its generic picker. Same reasoning as file-picker.ts: a list-filter typeahead the user still explicitly picks from, not a guessed dispatch decision, so not treated as a decision point. |
| `src/input/session-picker-modal.ts` | PORT | `SessionPickerModal`; deletion is explicitly redirected to the confirmed `/session delete <id> --yes` command rather than deleting inline, a deterministic safety rule, not a decision. |
| `src/input/settings-modal-agent-policy.ts` | PORT | Single re-export of `isAgentHiddenSettingKey` from `config/agent-settings-policy.ts`; no logic of its own. |
| `src/input/settings-modal-behavior.ts` | PORT | `modelPickerLaunchForKey` is a fixed seven-entry config-key-to-picker-flow map; `parseNumberEditBuffer` is a plain `Number()` parse. Deterministic. |

#### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/input/command-registry.ts`, `CommandRegistry.fuzzyMatch`/`scoreMatch` | A hand-rolled prefix-then-subsequence character scorer ranks every registered slash command against the user's partial `/`-prefixed text, for autocomplete suggestion order and for resolving which command Enter/Tab actually runs. | Jev dispatch over the closed set of registered commands (the "dispatch for closed-set commands" pattern the intent document names under engine routing), a choice reading over command ids instead of the character-scoring heuristic. |
| `src/input/model-picker-types.ts`, `detectFamily` | A twelve-entry regex ladder (`FAMILY_PATTERNS`) guesses a model's vendor family (Claude/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Qwen/GLM/MiniMax/DeepSeek/Mistral/Command/Grok/Kimi) from free-text substrings in its id/displayName, used only for the picker's `groupBy: 'family'` display grouping. | Pending the same owner ruling `docs/inventory/webui.md` requests for its identical `detectFamily`: either a Jev choice reading over the model's real catalog family (if the "no vendor names in routing or tier rules" prohibition is read as scoped to routing/tier, not display grouping), or the grouping feature is dropped outright if the owner reads that rule as unconditional. |

The second alphabetical half of `src/input` (excluding the ~90 agent-workspace files, covered by a separate slice): 13 source modules directly under `src/input/` (the settings-modal family, the setup-wizard receipts aggregator, the shell-passthrough runner, the slash-command parser, and the submission-intent/router pair) plus the 88 test files under `src/test/input/` whose alphabetical range falls in this half. All 101 files were read in full. None of them are WRFC-controller/workstream/attempt-judging code or QEMU-sandbox code, so this slice has zero DROP rows. Two files earn JEV, both flagged as owner-ruling or contract-runner decision points; everything else is deterministic state management, closed-set dispatch, structural test fixtures, or fixed-format/secret-shape checks that stay code per the project's "deterministic boundaries stay code" rule.

The recurring "secret-looking content" refusal exercised across several command tests (`agent-skills-command.test.ts`, `personas-command.test.ts`, `routines-command.test.ts`, `vibe-command.test.ts`) is a fixed key=value/token-shape scanner, the same kind of deterministic secret-shape check the intent document calls out for the gate's card-shape scanner; it is not a natural-language classification and stays PORT. Several `settings-modal*` files and tests carry a `wrfc` settings category, a `wrfc.scoreThreshold` key and a hidden `ui.wrfcMessages` key; these are literal config-schema plumbing (grouping/hiding a settings row), not guesswork, so they stay PORT, but the literal `wrfc` category name and any `/wrfc` command alias will need a mechanical rename to whatever the contract runner's settings surface is called once WRFC is replaced platform-wide; that rename is not itself a decision point.

Files: 101. PORT 99, JEV 2, HOIST 0, DROP 0. Decision points: 2.

| File | Disposition | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `src/input/settings-modal-daemon-writes.ts` | PORT | Routes a settings write to the connected host when the host owns the config key (`isDaemonOwnedConfigKey`), otherwise returns false so the local write path runs. Pure ownership-table lookup and promise plumbing, no guesswork. |
| `src/input/settings-modal-flag-entries.ts` | PORT | Builds the Features tab rows: prefers the live `FeatureFlagManager` state, derives from the domain settings key when unmanaged, sorted by domain then declaration order. Deterministic derivation over `FEATURE_SETTINGS`, no free-text classification. |
| `src/input/settings-modal-mcp-entries.ts` | PORT | Pure projection/copy of `McpRegistry.listServerSecurity()` into settings-modal row shape. No decision logic. |
| `src/input/settings-modal-secrets.ts` | PORT | Routes a secret-backed setting write to the daemon when the key is daemon-owned and a connected-host client exists, otherwise writes through `SecretsManager`. Same deterministic ownership-table routing as the daemon-writes sibling. |
| `src/input/settings-modal-subscriptions.ts` | PORT | Builds the Subscriptions tab rows from `SubscriptionManager`/service registry state; `determineFreshness` is a fixed-threshold time comparison (`expiresAt <= now`, `<= now + 24h`), arithmetic on a timestamp, not a judged reading. |
| `src/input/settings-modal-types.ts` | PORT | Types, category enum and `SETTINGS_CATEGORY_GROUPS` layout for the settings workspace. Includes a `'wrfc'` category member (grouped under "Advanced Runtime") purely as config-schema plumbing; carries over with only a mechanical rename once the contract runner's settings surface replaces WRFC's, not a decision point itself. |
| `src/input/settings-modal.ts` | PORT | The SettingsModal state class: category/setting navigation, inline edit, MCP trust-mode confirmation, subscription logout confirmation, flag toggling, payments CVV trade-off messaging. Every branch is deterministic state transition or config-schema lookup. References the same `'wrfc'` category (mechanical rename only, see settings-modal-types.ts). |
| `src/input/setup-wizard-live-receipts.ts` | PORT | Aggregates setup-wizard durable receipts from a fixed list of possible read-model paths on the command context, calling whichever snapshot/list method exists. Structural discovery over a static path list, no guesswork. |
| `src/input/shell-passthrough.ts` | PORT | Runs a user-typed `!<command>` shell escape via `Bun.spawn`, truncates output, and buffers a context block for the next turn. Deterministic subprocess execution and string truncation. |

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/test/input/model-picker.test.ts` (exercising `input/model-picker.ts`'s `detectFamily`) | A switch/regex ladder over a model's id/displayName text guessing its vendor family (`Claude`/`GPT`/`Gemini`/`DeepSeek`/`Other`), used only to group the model picker's "family" view | Flagged for the same owner ruling as `goodvibes-webui`'s `src/lib/model-catalog.ts` `detectFamily` (docs/inventory/webui.md): either a Jev choice-over-fixed-family-list reading, or dropping the family-grouping feature entirely if "no vendor or model names in routing or tier rules" is read to cover UI grouping too, since this is display-only and not a routing or tier decision |
| `src/test/input/delegation-runtime-command.test.ts` (exercising `/delegate`) | Submits a delegation as a structured shared-session message (goal, review-requested flag, success criteria, priority) directly to the old TUI/shared-session route (`operator.sessions.ensureSession`/`submitMessage`) | Per the owner ruling, delegation carries forward as JEV, not DROP: the same goal/acceptance-criteria/review-request fields become the contract runner's unit goal and acceptance criteria, with Jev judging the unit against those criteria the moment it completes, replacing the bare `reviewRequested` flag this test currently asserts on |

## Tools: harness (product tools over engine verbs)

This slice covers the `agent_harness` product tool family: the single giant mode-dispatch
tool (`agent-harness-tool.ts`), its schema/types/utils, and the roughly 90 supporting files
under `src/tools/` that each own one harness surface (autonomy, personal ops, learning
curator, model routing/readiness, memory, research, document ops, setup posture, workspace
actions/editors, remote peers, and more), plus the matching tests under `src/test/tools/`.
Architecturally almost everything here is a thin, deterministic dispatch/catalog layer that
calls down into engine verbs (`ToolRegistry`, `ProcessManager`, `ConfigManager`, work-plan and
artifact stores, etc.) that already live in the engine SDK; nothing in this slice needed to be
hoisted out to `packages/engine` on its own account. The decision-point density is real but
concentrated: it lives in the handful of files that turn free text or arbitrary metadata into a
typed judgment (model readiness/routing, learning-curator scoring, personal-ops lane
classification, autonomy intake/date-parts extraction, catalog/settings search relevance,
document reviewer readiness, execution-history/interactive-runtime classification). Everything
else is exact-match/case-insensitive/substring resolution chains, static catalogs, typed-field
dispatch, or deterministic security/redaction boundaries, and ports intact.

No file in this slice is WRFC-controller-specific or QEMU-sandbox-specific, including the four
`agent-harness-workspace-*-runner*.ts` / `agent-harness-workspace-editor-*.ts` files that the
original (voided) intent DROP bullet named. Read in full, they are generic action/editor
dispatch mechanics (typed `action.kind` / `editor.kind` switches, confirmation gating, tool-registry
handoffs) with no attempt-judging or sandbox logic in them, so per the owner's 2026-09-26 ruling
they PORT rather than DROP.

| Area | Files | PORT | JEV | HOIST | DROP | Decision points |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

#### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `agent-harness-model-readiness.ts` (`contextWindowScore`, `toolSupportScore`, `visionScore`, `costScore`, `privacyScore`, `latencyScore`, `scoreLocalModelRecipe`) | Hand-tuned point-additive scoring, e.g. `if (hardware.ramGb >= 16) { score += 12; ... } else { score -= 10; }` | Jev score reading (model readiness battery) |
| `agent-harness-model-routing.ts` | Which model route to recommend for a task, currently a heuristic dispatch | Jev routing/dispatch reading |
| `agent-harness-model-provider-health.ts` | Provider health status inferred from partial signals | Jev score reading (provider health battery) |
| `agent-harness-model-tool-catalog.ts` (`modelToolRelevance`) | Relevance ranking of model tools against a free-text query | Jev rerank/existence-check reading |
| `agent-harness-catalog-search.ts` (`searchCatalog`) | Two-tier strict-then-relaxed word match standing in for real relevance search (documented production incident: "spending limit"/"credit card" returned nothing against 32 live `payments.*` keys) | Jev rerank/existence-check reading |
| `agent-harness-settings-catalog.ts` (`SETTING_DOMAIN_VOCABULARY`) | Fixed keyword-to-domain vocabulary mapping user phrasing to settings domains | Jev rerank/existence-check reading |
| `agent-harness-mode-catalog.ts` (`harnessModeRelevance`) | Free-text phrase to harness-mode relevance scoring | Jev rerank/existence-check reading |
| `agent-harness-setup-plan.ts` (`setupRepairPlanCandidates`) | Candidate repair-plan ranking against setup-item free text | Jev rerank/existence-check reading |
| `agent-harness-operator-method-vocabulary.ts` | Fixed keyword vocabulary mapping phrasing to operator-method domains | Jev rerank/existence-check reading |
| `agent-harness-autonomy-intake.ts` (`detectSchedule`, `asksForEventTrigger`, `buildCandidates`) | Keyword-ladder classification of free text into schedule/reminder/routine/research/delegation/approval candidates, e.g. `hasAny(lower, ['audit','brief','briefing','check','daily report',...])` | Jev choice reading (intake classification battery) |
| `agent-harness-autonomy-watcher-read-models.ts` | Date-parts extraction from free-text/metadata into schedule date components | Jev fidelity-check / entity-alignment reading |
| `agent-harness-personal-ops-discovery.ts` (`toolCapability`) | Regex classification of arbitrary tool descriptions into capability tags, e.g. `if (/(send|reply|draft|compose|archive|label|delete|trash|mark|move)/.test(text)) return record('confirmed-effect', 'inbox-write')` | Jev choice reading (capability classification battery) |
| `agent-harness-personal-ops-intake.ts` (`buildPersonalOpsIntakeCandidates`) | Keyword-ladder classification of free text into inbox/calendar/task candidates | Jev choice reading (intake classification battery) |
| `agent-harness-personal-ops-lanes.ts` | Personal Ops lane classification (which lane a request/record belongs to) | Jev choice reading (lane classification battery) |
| `agent-harness-learning-curator-common.ts` (`itemUsefulness`, `itemFreshness`, `itemSourceQuality`, `itemRisk`) | Point-additive scoring, e.g. `const triggerSignal = Math.min(20, item.triggers.length * 5); ... return clampScore(35 + triggerSignal + tagSignal + usageSignal + confidenceSignal)` | Jev score reading (learning curator battery) |
| `agent-harness-learning-curator-consolidation.ts` (`normalizeDuplicateKey`) | Duplicate grouping by normalized-name string equality | Jev entity-alignment reading |
| `agent-harness-learning-curator-proposals.ts` (`inferMemoryClass`) | Regex classification of free text into decision/constraint/preference memory classes | Jev choice reading |
| `agent-harness-learning-auto-promote.ts` | Threshold-driven promote/consolidate decision built on the curator scores above | Jev yes/no reading gating the promotion action |
| `agent-harness-memory-refinement.ts` | Judgment over which knowledge gaps warrant semantic refinement | Jev score/choice reading |
| `agent-harness-execution-history.ts` (`VERIFICATION_PATTERN`, `looksLikeVerificationRecord`) | Regex guess of whether a free-text execution record represents a real verification/test-run event | Jev yes/no reading |
| `agent-harness-interactive-runtime-records.ts` (`runtimeKind`) | Regex classification of raw runtime metadata into pty-session/sudo-mediation/etc, e.g. `if (/pty|terminal-session|interactive-session/.test(raw)) return 'pty-session'; if (/sudo|credential|privilege/.test(raw)) return 'sudo-mediation'` | Jev choice reading feeding the gate's exec-risk classification |
| `agent-harness-browser-control.ts` (`selectBrowserControlWorkflow`) | Regex scoring of free text against browser/screenshot/desktop-control workflows | Jev routing/dispatch reading |
| `agent-harness-browser-pwa-read-models.ts` (`evidenceBoolean`) | Free-text keyword fallback (`mobile`/`touch`/`responsive`) when explicit capability fields are absent | Jev yes/no reading |
| `agent-harness-document-ops-reviewer-readiness.ts` (`buildReviewerReadinessChecklist`) | Checklist-style readiness verification before export/handoff | Jev policy-checklist reading |
| `agent-harness-process-timeout-policy.ts` (borderline, kept PORT) | Fixed program-name allow-list for long-lived-process timeout policy | Stays code (deterministic execution-timeout boundary); flagged for re-check if the list starts guessing from free text rather than exact names |

## Tools: other agent tools (route planner, policy, registries-facing tools)

This slice covers 91 files: all of them are test files under `src/test/tools/` (and `src/test/tools/shared/`) that were partitioned into this slice as the "tests" half of the products/agent tools inventory (the companion source-file slice, `tools-other-b`, covers the matching `src/tools/*.ts` files; the harness family, `agent-harness-*`, is a separate already-completed slice). Every disposition below is based on reading each test file in full and judging what it proves about the source module it exercises, the same way `docs/inventory/webui.md` disposes end-to-end and integration tests by what they pin rather than by file name alone. Two files here (`agent-route-tool.test.ts` and `agent.test.ts`) pin the two biggest decision points in the whole agent product: the route planner's keyword ladder, and the WRFC sub-agent orchestration controller.

Files: 91. PORT 40, JEV 30, HOIST 21, DROP 0.

Bucket member of the intent's "personal ops harness, Google and accounts tools" HOIST group; this test itself exercises only the deterministic trust boundary, not the lane-classification decision point named for that group. |

Deterministic string/registry matching; it proves route labels are not phantom, it does not itself judge anything. |

This is the write-review-fix-check loop and attempt-judging machinery the project's owner ruling says becomes "JEV in the contract runner" (sub-agents are kept, per the standing ruling; WRFC-controller code itself is not simply dropped). The same file also carries `agent-tool-policy-guard.ts`'s large deterministic allow/deny-list tests (exec foreground-only, read-only modes for remote/channel/mcp/fetch/state/control/analyze/task/team/worklist/packet/query) which is the separate, deterministic "tool policy guard... to the gate" HOIST bullet bundled into the same test file. See decision-points table. Its tool-policy-guard tests are ported with the guard as `packages/engine/test/gate-tool-policy-guard.test.ts` and `gate-tool-policy-guard-reads.test.ts`. |

One call-out: the `wrfc` preset specifically encodes the WRFC gather/plan/apply/review/revision/complete loop by name; per the project's WRFC-specific DROP rule that named preset (not the generic engine or its sibling presets) should not survive under that name once the contract runner replaces the WRFC loop project-wide. |

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `agent-route-tool.ts` (tested by `agent-route-tool.test.ts`), the whole-file `route` planner | A hand-built keyword ladder over a large fixed catalog of route ids: free-text queries are matched word-by-word against per-route trigger phrases with a numeric relevance `score`, disambiguating e.g. "take a screenshot of the screen" (desktop) from "fill out the contact form... submit it" (browser), and "run claude code with pty=true and handle sudo prompts" (interactive-process-capability) from "write process documentation" (not that route) purely by wording overlap. | Hoisted in E.4 as the engine task route planner: `routing.task-route.pick`, a Choice over the route catalog plus one Noul fit question per route in one request, with the slots battery and the named-id selector in the same planning pass (packages/engine/sdk/src/platform/routing/task-routes/). |
| `agent-tool.ts` / `WrfcController` (from `@pellux/goodvibes-sdk/platform/agents`, tested by `agent.test.ts`) | The WRFC controller's write-review-fix-check loop and attempt judging: `reviewMode:"wrfc"` plus `template:"reviewer"` triggers ad hoc owner-chain role assignment (`wrfcRole:"owner"`, `wrfcRouteReason:"root-review-role-normalized"`) and collapses a multi-agent WRFC fan-out into one authoritative chain, tracked as free-standing state on each agent record rather than as judged, quantized work. | The contract runner: goal + acceptance criteria set once, decomposed into units/groups judged by Jev the moment each completes, with best-of-N as candidate selection instead of a WRFC review/revision state machine. Sub-agents are kept as the execution substrate (per the standing project ruling); only the WRFC-controller state machine and its owner-chain heuristics are retired. |
| `agent-tool-policy-guard.ts` (tested inside `agent.test.ts`) | A large, hand-maintained allow/deny list per tool name (exec limited to foreground/serial; read-only mode enumerations for remote/channel/mcp/fetch/state/control/analyze/registry/find/web_search/task/team/worklist/packet/query), each enforced by exact string/enum matching. | Named in intent as "The tool policy guard and every policy and posture tool, to the gate": HOIST. E.5 reading (2026-09-27 ruling): the mode enumerations stay code because they are the Agent product's declaration of which modes its main-conversation tools offer (a tool schema the product publishes, not a reading of any call's content); the checks inside that did judge content (secret paths for read, hazardous settings keys, the confirmation text) are Jev readings. |
| `agent-model-compare-tool.ts` (tested by `agent-model-compare-tool.test.ts`), `mode:"judge"` | A prompt-and-parse judge: the calling model reads two blind candidate outputs itself and reports a free-text `winnerBlindId` + `reasons` through the tool call, with no structured rubric or independent evaluation. | Intent's explicit "Model comparison judgment is JEV, with candidate selection replacing the prompt-and-parse judge": a Jev choice/rerank pattern over the two (or N) blinded candidates against the supplied rubric. |
| `agent-research-sources-tool.ts` (tested by `agent-research-sources-tool.test.ts`), `add`/`review` actions | `credibility` (`high`/`medium`/`low`) and a numeric `score` are supplied directly by the calling model as unstructured judgment on each captured source, with no evaluation step in between. | Intent's explicit "research-source credibility scored": a Jev score reading (rubric-scored credibility) replacing the model's free-typed value. |
| `agent-harness-process-timeout-policy.ts`'s `resolveBackgroundProcessClass` (tested by `agent-terminal-timeout-contract.test.ts`) | A hardcoded keyword list of known GUI/browser binary names (`brave`, `firefox`, `google-chrome`, `chromium-browser`, `code`, `xdg-open`) matched against a command's leading token to decide `long_lived` (never killed by a routine timeout) vs. `command` (killed as usual); an explicit `processClass` override exists in both directions. | A Jev classify/choice reading over "is this command starting a long-lived, user-facing application vs. a short automated command," matching intent's "execution, process, command... tools" HOIST-with-gate bucket rather than a fixed binary-name list that misses any GUI app not on it. |
| `agent-harness-operator-methods.ts`'s catalog search (tested by `operator-method-catalog-search.test.ts`) | A hand-tuned, multi-tier text matcher: exact phrase, then per-word AND across id/title/description, then a "relaxed" single-word fallback with a "near misses" note, plus an "ambiguous" branch when a plain word matches many methods, replacing an earlier, even cruder "one contiguous substring over a collapsed label" matcher that a real incident showed returned zero results for `query:"google"` against 434 methods. | A Jev existence-check/rerank pattern over the operator-method catalog (structured relevance reading) instead of a hand-tuned word-overlap ladder with ad hoc fallback tiers. |
| `@pellux/goodvibes-sdk` registry tool's `mode:"recommend"` (tested by `registry-tool.test.ts`) | "Sorts results by keyword relevance to task": ranks skills/agents/tools against a free-text `task` string by naive keyword/description overlap (e.g. ranking `code-review` above `test-driven` for "review code quality"). | Matches intent's engine "skills | Skill documents, progressive disclosure, CRUD | JEV: relevance by rerank": a Jev rerank pattern over the registry entries against the task description. |
| `agent-vibe-tool.ts`, `action:"import_persona"` (tested by `agent-vibe-tool.test.ts`) | An arbitrary, user-authored VIBE.md file's name/description/body is adopted directly into an active, reviewed persona on `confirm:true` + `explicitUserRequest` alone; no content screening step is visible before the persona becomes active. | Intent's explicit "The vibe file and vibe confirmation routes also carry over; they are screened by the engine policy checklist, which is a JEV use": a Jev policy-checklist pattern screening imported persona content before it can become active. |
| `agent-work-plan-tool.ts` / `agent-workspace-tool.ts` / `agent-delegation-tool.ts` / `agent-review-packet-presets-tool.ts` / `agent-review-packet-share-tool.ts` (the DROP-voided bucket) | Ad hoc dispatch of work items to sub-agents via freeform `agent { mode: "spawn" }` / `batch-spawn` calls with manually authored `successCriteria`/`context` strings, plus self-authored preset/receipt artifacts (freshness judged only by artifact `createdAt` comparison) standing in for structured, judged units of work. | Per the owner ruling of 2026-09-26 ("the DROP-voided delegation/work-plan/workspace/review-packet tools... become JEV onto the contract runner... NOT DROP"): these tools submit contract-runner verbs (units/groups/deliverables judged by Jev as they complete) instead of ad hoc `agent`-tool dispatch and self-authored presets. |
| `agent-accounts-tool.ts` / `agent-google-tool.ts` (the "personal ops harness, Google and accounts tools" bucket; tested by `agent-accounts-tool.test.ts`, `agent-google-connect-actions.test.ts`, `agent-google-outward-effects.test.ts`, `agent-google-verification.test.ts`, `agent-personal-ops-tool.test.ts`) | Intent names "lane classification is a choice" for this bucket as its guessed decision point, but none of these five test files directly exercises a lane-classification heuristic; each one instead pins a deterministic trust boundary, OAuth-completion path, or dispatch table. | A Jev choice reading over personal-ops "lanes" (inbox/tasks/calendar/etc.), per intent's own naming; flagged here as evidence-thin for this specific slice of tests, since the actual heuristic lives in source modules (`agent-accounts-tool.ts`'s lane assignment, or the harness personal-ops modules) not directly covered by these test files. |
| `agent-autonomy-schedule-tool.ts` / `agent-reminder-schedule-tool.ts` / `agent-schedule-tool.ts` / `agent-schedule-edit-tool.ts` / `agent-autonomy-tool.ts` (the "autonomy intake, queue and watchers, and the schedule and reminder tools" bucket) | Intent names "date-parts extraction" as the guessed decision point for this bucket (turning natural-language time expressions like "every day" or "tomorrow at 9am" into schedule fields), but every test in this slice supplies already-structured `scheduleKind`/`scheduleValue`/`cron` fields rather than natural language, so the NL parsing itself is not directly exercised by these five test files. | A Jev date-parts reading (structured extraction of kind/interval/timezone from natural language), per intent's own naming and the matching TUI bullet ("To the engine scheduler, as a JEV date-parts reading: natural-language schedule parsing"); flagged here as evidence-thin for this specific slice. |

The second alphabetical half of `src/tools/*.ts` in goodvibes-agent, excluding the `agent-harness-*` family (a separate, already-completed slice). This batch is dominated by two things: a large family of thin routing facades that translate a small closed-set `action`/`mode` alias into a call on `agent_harness` (dispatch over a known vocabulary, not guesswork, so most stay PORT or HOIST depending on whether the underlying subsystem is agent-specific or platform-generic), and the route planner (`agent-route-planner*`), which is the single largest concentration of keyword-ladder guesswork in this slice and is explicitly named in the intent document as the JEV target for the route tool. The delegation/work-plan/workspace/vibe/review-packet tools are JEV per the owner ruling of 2026-09-26 (they submit contract-runner verbs, not DROP). 90 files: 21 PORT, 18 JEV, 51 HOIST, 0 DROP (the six route planner and route tool files were hoisted into engine routing in E.4).

#### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `agent-route-planner-helpers.ts` (dozens of functions, e.g. `scheduleLike` L25-43, `autonomousLike` L45-60, `mediaGenerationLike` L75-79, `browserControlLike` L81-110, `personalOpsLike` L289-315, `personalOpsLaneFromText` L317-326, `securityPermissionLike`/`securityStatusLike`/`securityFindingLike`/`securityPolicyExplainLike` L485-573, `channelSetupLike`/`channelTriageLike`/`channelDeliveriesLike` L382-396, `modelRouteReadinessLike` L467-483) | Hundreds of literal substring/`hasAny`/`hasAll` checks against a lowercased free-text user request, classifying it into a route category, lane, or effect posture | Hoisted in E.4: every predicate is replaced by the `routing.task-route.slots` yes/no and closed-vocabulary questions and the `routing.task-route.named-id` selector (task-routes/slots.ts, named-ids.ts). |
| `agent-route-planner.ts`, `buildCandidates`/`confidence` (L62-103) | Every keyword-matched candidate gets a hand-tuned integer score (35-100); candidates are sorted by score and the top one becomes "preferred" | Hoisted in E.4: the preferred route is the pick when its fit reads yes, alternatives are the other fitting routes by fit probability, and confidence comes from the reading outcome (task-routes/planner.ts, route-selector.ts). |
| `agent-route-planner-candidates-setup.ts` (whole file, calls into helpers above) | Applies the keyword-ladder predicates to decide setup/host-diagnostics/settings-import/settings/model-provider/personality/memory/personal-ops route candidates and their confidence | Hoisted in E.4: the routes are catalog entries (task-routes/catalog-setup.ts) chosen by `routing.task-route.pick`. |
| `agent-route-planner-candidates-surfaces.ts` (whole file) | Applies the keyword-ladder predicates to decide browser-control/media-generation/voice/TTS/browser-cockpit/channel/documents/knowledge/security/support-bundle/session/release-audit route candidates | Hoisted in E.4: the routes are catalog entries (task-routes/catalog-surfaces.ts) chosen by `routing.task-route.pick`. |
| `agent-route-planner-candidates-work.ts` (whole file) | Applies the keyword-ladder predicates to decide research/schedule/autonomy/file-recovery/interactive-process/process-lifecycle/delegated-build route candidates | Hoisted in E.4: the routes are catalog entries (task-routes/catalog-work.ts) chosen by `routing.task-route.pick`. |
| `agent-route-tool.ts`, `execute` (L94-98) | Exposes the keyword-ladder planner above as a model-callable `route action:"plan"` | Hoisted in E.4: `createTaskRouteTool` calls `planTaskRoute` (task-routes/tool.ts). |
| `agent-platform-boundary-policy.ts`, `ownerAskedAboutPlatformSource` (L125-135) | Three regex word-lists (`SOURCE_WORDS`, `PLATFORM_ATTENTION_WORDS`, `PLATFORM_NAME_WORDS`) test whether the user's message this turn "asked about" the GoodVibes platform source, versus the agent reaching for it unprompted | JEV yes/no existence-check reading |
| `agent-policy-explanation.ts`, `resolveToolName` (L127-159) | Exact match, then case-insensitive match, then substring-of-description search over every registered tool definition to resolve a free-text lookup target to one tool, with an "ambiguous" candidate list on multiple matches | JEV entity alignment / choice pattern |
| `agent-model-compare-types.ts`, `SYNTHESIS_THEMES` (L181-191), consumed by `agent-model-compare-judgment.ts`'s `formatComparisonSynthesis` | A six-entry regex-pattern ladder (e.g. `/concrete\|actionable\|specific.../i`) classifying a human judge's free-text `reasons`/`notes` into cross-session preference themes | JEV rerank/choice pattern classifying judgment reasoning against a fixed theme taxonomy |
| `agent-model-compare-run.ts`, `resolveRequestedModels` (L58-85) | Progressively looser string matching (exact registryKey, then `provider:model`, then modelId, then displayName, then substring) to resolve a user-typed model reference to one catalog entry | JEV entity alignment pattern |
| `agent-delegation-tool.ts` (facade; heuristic lives in the already-covered harness) | A harness-side heuristic chooses local-first vs. delegated/isolated/remote execution for a task with no acceptance-criteria judgment of the outcome | Contract-runner group/unit assignment (JEV routing pattern), per the owner ruling |
| `agent-work-plan-tool.ts`, `createAgentWorkPlanTool`/`dispatchAgentsFromWorkPlan` (whole file) | Local `WorkPlanStore` CRUD plus ad-hoc agent spawn (`agent` tool `spawn`/`batch-spawn`); "done" is whatever the dispatched agent self-reports, with no judged acceptance criteria | Contract-runner unit/group submission judged against stated acceptance criteria (JEV verification reading), per the owner ruling |
| `agent-workspace-tool.ts` (whole file) | Workspace actions execute directly behind only a boolean `confirm` gate; no judgment of whether the action's result actually satisfied the request | Contract-runner verb submission with Jev-judged unit completion, per the owner ruling |
| `agent-vibe-tool.ts`, `import_persona`/`init` (L191-284) | VIBE.md import/creation is gated only by `confirm:true` + `explicitUserRequest` plus a secret-content scan; the imported personality text itself is never screened for policy fit | JEV policy-checklist pattern screening vibe drafts/imports, per the owner ruling |
| `agent-review-packet-presets-core.ts`, `replacementForRole`/`auditPresetFreshness` (L489-577, 603-678) | Review-packet composition is assembled from freestanding artifact ids the caller must supply and track by hand, with exact-metadata-match "freshness" auditing standing in for any judgment of whether the packet's evidence is still adequate | Contract-runner packet submission tied to judged unit/group evidence, per the owner ruling |
| `agent-personal-ops-tool.ts` (facade; classification lives in `agent-route-planner-helpers.ts`) | The intent's "lane classification is a choice" capability is realized by `personalOpsLaneFromText`'s keyword ladder in the route planner, not inside this tool | Same JEV choice pattern as the route-planner rows above |

## Test: cross-cutting miscellany

This slice covers 84 files drawn from `src/test/acp`, `src/test/agents`, `src/test/artifacts`, `src/test/automation`, `src/test/browser`, `src/test/channels`, `src/test/chaos`, `src/test/contracts`, `src/test/control-plane`, `src/test/daemon`, `src/test/deps`, `src/test/docs`, `src/test/features`, `src/test/fixtures`, `src/test/git`, `src/test/helpers`, `src/test/hooks` and `src/test/integration`, the alphabetically first half of a cross-cutting test miscellany. Since the directory name for a test file rarely names its real subject, every file was read in full and classified by what it actually imports and exercises (the SDK/engine module under test, or the local runtime/product module), not by its folder. Where a file imports from `@pellux/goodvibes-sdk/platform/*`, that subpath was matched against the intent document's engine subsystem table to find its disposition; where a file exercises this repo's own `src/runtime`, `src/agent`, `src/tools` etc., the matching product-level PORT/JEV/HOIST/DROP call in the intent document's agent-product section was used instead. Test helpers and non-`.test.ts` fixtures (spawned child-process probes, TS hook fixtures, OpenClaw import fixtures) were classified by the subject of the module or feature they support.

Counts: 84 files. PORT 75, JEV 7, HOIST 2, DROP 0.

#### Files

| File | Disposition | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `src/test/acp/acp.test.ts` | PORT | Imports `AcpConnection`/`AcpManager` from `platform/acp`. Exercises spawn/cancel/error-handling/waitAll over the agent control protocol, all deterministic process and event-bus mechanics. The intent's engine table lists "discovery, mcp, plugins, acp" as PORT; nothing here is WRFC- or QEMU-specific. |
| `src/test/agents/archetypes.test.ts` | PORT | Imports `ArchetypeLoader` from `platform/agents`. Tests frontmatter parsing, built-in archetype listing, override merging, all deterministic file/YAML parsing. Matches the intent's "profiles, templates \| Named config profiles; agent archetypes \| PORT". |
| `src/test/agents/context-window.test.ts` | JEV | Imports `AgentOrchestrator`, `estimateTokens`, `compactSmallWindow`, `isContextSizeExceededError` from `platform/core`. `isContextSizeExceededError` is a keyword/regex ladder over free-text provider error messages ("context_length_exceeded", "prompt is too long", "tokens exceed", etc.) deciding whether to trigger a compaction retry, exactly the guesswork-over-prose pattern Jev replaces. The intent's "core" row is JEV ("compaction keeps a fidelity check"); this file's char-count token estimate and 85% threshold stay arithmetic/code. |
| `src/test/agents/message-bus.test.ts` | PORT | Imports `AgentMessageBus` from `platform/agents`. Tests send/broadcast/subscribe/TTL and a fixed role-based communication policy (direct-route and broadcast allow/deny by registered role). Deterministic policy table, no guesswork; sub-agents and their communication are kept per the owner ruling. |

#### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/test/agents/context-window.test.ts` (`isContextSizeExceededError`, `platform/core`) | A keyword/regex ladder over free-text provider error messages ("context_length_exceeded", "prompt is too long", "tokens exceed", etc.) decides whether a failure is a context-size overrun worth a compaction retry. | A Jev yes/no reading over the error text, run once before the existing retry-once mechanics, with no fallback to the old ladder. |
| `src/test/agents/orchestrator.test.ts` (`AgentOrchestrator`'s turn loop and background-agent permission gate) | The turn loop is plain code driven by a fixed `reviewMode: 'wrfc'` record field; the background-tool-call ask is gated by a fixed `permissions.mode` string (`prompt`/`allow-all`/etc.). | The loop becomes a per-unit contract-runner run judged as it happens; the permission mode becomes a preset over the engine gate's stakes table. |
| `src/test/agents/score-extraction.test.ts` (`extractScoreFromText`/`extractPassedFromText`/`extractIssuesFromText`, `platform/agents`) | Regex ladders read a free-text review reply for a "Score: X/10" number, "passed"/"approved"/"failed" keyword language, and `[severity] description` issue bullets. | A Jev score-on-a-rubric reading and a yes/no verdict reading for pass/fail, plus a policy-checklist reading for the issue list, run directly against the review text. |
| `src/test/contracts/permission-decisions.test.ts` (`LayeredPolicyEvaluator`'s Layer 2 mode constraints) | A fixed enum of session modes (`default`/`plan`/`allow-all`/`custom`/`background-restricted`/`remote-restricted`) gates every tool call at Layer 2. | Gate presets over the engine's stakes table. (Layer 1's destructive-prefix/dangerous-pattern/path-escape/destructive-SQL safety checks stay deterministic code, per the gate's own carve-out.) |
| `src/test/daemon/occasions-push-pull-no-double-speak.test.ts` (`occasions.answer`'s interview trigger) | A fixed occasion-type string (`gift-giving`) answered `yes` decides whether the short interview opens. | The occasions engine's solemn-or-celebratory gated choice (an upstream Jev reading); this file proves the agent's own push destination and pull-quiet handshake keep working once that reading is in place. |
| `src/test/helpers/runtime-services.ts` (`getTestWrfcController`/`initTestWrfcController`) | The helper wires and exposes the old `WrfcController` (attempt scoring/pass-fail judging) for every dependent test file to reach. | A renamed accessor onto the contract runner's per-unit Jev check (per the WRFC-to-Jev mapping in `docs/inventory/wrfc-to-jev.md`); every caller of `getTestWrfcController`/`initTestWrfcController` needs updating in lockstep. |

This slice covers 83 files, the alphabetically second half of a cross-cutting test miscellany
under `src/test/` in goodvibes-agent (integration, integrations, intelligence, knowledge, mcp,
media, multimodal, plugins, release-gates, scheduler, scripts, security, sessions, setup.ts,
state, ux, watchers, web-search, work-plans, workflow). None of these directory names are
themselves engine subsystems, so for every file the method was the same: read the file in full,
find what it actually imports and exercises (an SDK `platform/*` module, this repo's own
`@/runtime`, or a plain build/CI script), and give the file the disposition that real subject has
per the intent document's engine subsystem table and the owner ruling voiding the work-plan/
delegation/orchestration DROP lines. Where a subsystem is PORT overall but a specific file's own
code contains a guessed classification (a keyword/substring ladder over free text, a fixed
numeric quality threshold, a risk/trust label attached to unstructured input), that file is
marked JEV and a decision-points row is added; where a file only exercises the deterministic,
already-PORT parts of an otherwise-JEV subsystem (plain parsing, CRUD, contract-shape checks over
a JEV surface), it is marked JEV or PORT per the subsystem call below but gets no decision-points
row of its own, the same restraint `docs/inventory/webui.md` uses for its contract-tree view
files. Three files test the agent's own capability/trust/card-intake surfaces that the intent
document explicitly lists as HOIST targets into the engine; none of the 83 files touch anything
WRFC-controller-only or QEMU-sandbox-only, so this slice has zero DROP rows.

Files: 83. PORT 60, JEV 20, HOIST 3, DROP 0. Decision points: 9.

| File | Disposition | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `src/test/integration/config-persistence.test.ts` | PORT | Imports `ConfigManager` from this repo's own `../../config/index.ts`. Exercises plain get/set roundtrips (booleans, `permissions.mode` string enum, `provider.model`, `display.*`) with no interpretation of free text; config subsystem is PORT. |
| `src/test/integration/hook-lifecycle.test.ts` | PORT | Imports `HookDispatcher`/`HookEvent`/`HookResult` from `platform/hooks`. Exercises fire()/register() and Pre/Post/Fail phase transitions on fixed enum fields; hooks are PORT and "model-free by design" per the engine table. |
| `src/test/integration/permission-flow.test.ts` | JEV | Imports `PermissionManager` from `platform/permissions` and this repo's own `PolicyRuntimeState`. Exercises the `permissions.mode` enum (prompt/allow-all/custom) and per-tool allow/prompt/deny gating end to end; this is exactly the permission-mode decision the gate subsystem turns into presets over a stakes table, so it is a decision point (see table). |
| `src/test/integration/tool-execution.test.ts` | JEV | Imports `ToolRegistry` (PORT tools) and `PermissionManager`/`createPermissionConfigReader` (gate) together, driving the full register-tool -> permission-check -> execute -> emit pipeline. The permission-mode/auto-approve gating half of this pipeline is the same decision point as `permission-flow.test.ts`. |
| `src/test/integrations/delivery.test.ts` | JEV | Imports `DeliveryQueue`/`classifyDeliveryError`/`snapshotQueueStatus` from `platform/integrations`. `classifyDeliveryError` is a substring/status-code ladder over free-text error messages ("timeout", "aborted", "ECONNREFUSED", HTTP 4xx/5xx) defaulting unknown errors to retryable, guesswork of exactly the kind the intent document's goal statement calls out for replacement. |

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `PermissionManager`/`permissions.mode` (exercised in `integration/permission-flow.test.ts`, `integration/tool-execution.test.ts`, `security/permission-audit.test.ts`) | A three-way mode enum (prompt/allow-all/custom) plus a fixed per-tool action table (allow/prompt/deny/delegate) picked by the operator, with no graduated sense of stakes | Gate presets over a stakes table (graduated autonomy), per the engine gate row |
| `classifyDeliveryError` (`integrations/delivery.test.ts`) | A substring/HTTP-status ladder over free-text error messages ("timeout", "aborted", "ECONNREFUSED", 4xx/5xx), defaulting unknown errors to retryable | A Jev yes/no reading of retryable-vs-terminal, no keyword fallback |
| `GitHubIntegration.eventToPrompt`'s `issue_comment` handling (`integrations/github.test.ts`) | A substring check for whether a comment body mentions/addresses the bot ("@goodvibes") before generating a prompt | A Jev intent-to-handler reading (does this message address the agent) |
| Knowledge ingest acceptance and review scoring (`knowledge/graphql.test.ts`, `knowledge/knowledge-api.test.ts`, `knowledge/service.test.ts`) | Ingested sources are accepted on successful parse/fetch alone, with `review.confidence`/`trustTier` set as plain input data rather than computed | Jev gated writes and quality scoring on a specificity rubric before acceptance |
| Knowledge consolidation candidates/reports (`knowledge/graphql.test.ts`, `knowledge/service.test.ts`, light/deep `runJob` calls) | Fixed job logic classifies and groups consolidation candidates | Jev duplicate detection by entity alignment and usefulness scoring |
| MCP server `trustMode`/risk classification (`mcp/mcp-api.test.ts`, `mcp/registry.test.ts`, `release-gates/runtime-knowledge-security-gate.test.ts`'s `buildMcpAttackPathReview`) | A fixed label (`ask-on-risk`, `riskLevel: 'critical'`) attached to a server/tool call to decide isolation, quarantine, and approval | Gate's side-effect, risk-family and sandbox-advisory batteries |
| `classifyFailure` (`release-gates/operability-gate.test.ts`) | A precedence-ordered ladder over structured flags plus free-text `errorMessage` substrings ("timed out", "API error", "network failure"), defaulting to `unknown` | A Jev choice reading over the closed `FailureClass` set |
| `computeQualityScore`/`LOW_QUALITY_THRESHOLD` (`release-gates/performance-gate.test.ts`) | Compaction output quality scored by a fixed numeric threshold (0.4) to decide strategy escalation | Jev's fidelity-check pattern for compaction quality |
| Knowledge injection `ingestMode` (`release-gates/runtime-knowledge-security-gate.test.ts`'s `buildKnowledgeInjectionPrompt`, `ingestMode: 'keyword-ranked'`) | Retrieved knowledge is ranked into the prompt by a named keyword-ranking mode | Jev's rerank and existence-check pattern on retrieval |
